 8.3  Takeoff Procedure 
  
      All takeoff paths complying with the airspeed-height diagram and with 
      aircraft performance limitations are authorized. 
  
      NOTE : When the airspeed reading is beyond the blue mark corresponding 
             to the minimum level flight speed at OEI LO rating, the flight      
             can proceed without loss of height, even if an engine fails. 
  
 
8.4  Recommended Takeoff Procedure (Refer to takeoff distances in Section 5 
                                   Performance Data). 
8.4.1 For Gross Weight up to the maximum twin engine hover performance          
      IGE 10 ft (Section 5.1 Fig 3a)                                            
  
      This procedure is applicable on level ground with unobstructed takeoff 
      path. 
      - Hover into the wind (if appropriate) at a height of 10 ft. 
      - Move the cyclic stick forward from the hover attitude to a 10° 
        nose-down attitude in about 2 seconds. 
      - Allow the aircraft to accelerate, adjusting the collective pitch to 
        initially maintain a roughly constant height. 
        Keep a constant nose-down attitude. 
      - When the airspeed reaches V1, rotate the fuselage and adopt the 
        aircraft attitude to climb at 45 knots (TAS) or more. 
      - Set NR + 10 to..................................Off 
      - Control power as necessary while monitoring the first limit indicator. 
      - Increase IAS to Vy. 
  
8.4.2 For Gross Weight up to the maximum twin engine hover performance          
      IGE 5 ft (Section 5.1 Fig 3b)                                             
                                                                                
      This procedure is applicable on a flat strip of at least 400m (1310 ft).   
      Follow the same procedure in 8.4.1 but :                                   
      - Hover IGE at 5 ft.                                                       
      - Limit the pitch down attitude change to a maximum of 5° in               
        not less than 2 seconds.                                                 
      - Use cyclic stick and collective pitch to maintain the height during      
        the initial acceleration.                                                
  
8.4.3 Running Takeoff                                                           
  
      At high gross weight, the following procedure is recommended as an        
      alternate to the normal takeoff procedure.                                
      Aircraft aligned with the strip axis.                                     
      Cyclic stick centered, gradually apply the maximum continuous power.      
      Let the aircraft accelerate by controlling the roll with the cyclic       
      stick and the yaw with the tail rotor control pedals.                     
      When the aircraft lifts off, adjust the power without exceeding the       
      maximum takeoff power and continue accelerating up to Vy,                 
      staying near the ground.  
 
8.5 Check after Takeoff                                                        R 
                                                                                
    Refer to SUP.45 for aircraft equipped with ELECTRONIC WARFARE SYSTEM (EWS) R 
 
 
                                                                               R
 

PDF PAGE 341
   
 
FLIGHT MANUAL 
  
   
9  CLIMBING   
   
   - Landing gear ..................................... Retract (see Note 1)  
     . L/G indicator lights............................ Off  
     . MPAI CLOSED lights (if SAND FILTERS switch in   
       AUTO L/G position) ............................. Off  
   
   NOTE 1  : If wheel brakes were used heavily on the ground before takeoff,  
            leave the landing gear extended for at least 5 minutes.  
   
   - Bleed valve ...................................... Normal threshold  
     . Both indicator lights .......................... Off  
   
   - Power ............................................ As required (first  
                                                        limit indicator)  
   
   - Torque, Ng and t4 readings ....................... Monitor  
   
   - Heating and demisting systems .................... As required  
   
   - Recommended climbing speed ....................... Vy  
   
   - MPAI ............................................. As required  
                                                        . Refer to paragraph 8  
   
   NOTE 2  : After a flight in snow or through an icing area, it is   
            recommended not to open the bullets. However, should this  
            be required, proceed as follows :  
            - Fly for at least 15 minutes at OAT well above freezing   
              (not lower than +5°C).  
            - Check that there is no visible accretion of ice on the aircraft.  
            - Open one of the two air intakes.  
            - Open the other air intake five minutes after the first one, if   
              the first engine has been operating properly during that period   
              of time.  
   
10  LEVEL FLIGHT   
   
    Twin-Engine Level Flight   
   
    - Normal cruising rating .......................... As required  
   
    - Fast cruising rating ............................ Max continuous  
                                                        rating on FLI  
    - Maximum range rating ............................ 135 kt (TAS)  
                                                        approx. with no   
                                                        wind    
   
    - MPAI ............................................ As required  
                                                        . Refer to paragraph 8  
                                                           
   
   
   
   
   
   
                                                                               R

                   04-46 Page 19
 *01*

FLIGHT MANUAL 
  
   
11  DESCENT   
   
    - Disengage ALT mode or substitue a descent mode.  
   
    - Reduce collective pitch to begin descent (use AFCS descent mode if  
      necessary).  
   
    - Check the following (especially at high rates of descent) :  
      . Rotor speed ....................................... less than 290 rpm  
                                                            (109.5 %)            
      . Airspeed .......................................... less than VNE  
   
    NOTE 1  : At altitudes above about 13100 ft (4000 m) the free turbine         
             speed may be identical with the corresponding rotor speed up  
             to the maximum transient power-off rotor speed of 310 rpm (117 %)   
             In this case, increase the collective pitch slightly to reduce  
             NR and NF to a maximum of 290 rpm (109.5 %).                        
             Maximum permissible duration : 20 seconds above 290 rpm (109.5 %)   
   
    NOTE 2  : Minimum rate of descent at any pitch setting is obtained at Vy.  
   
    - MPAI ................................................ As required  
                                                            . Refer to   
                                                              paragraph 8  
   
12  APPROACH - LANDING   
   
12.1  Checks Prior to Landing   
   
      - Landing gear ...................................... Down  
        . Three green indicator lights .................... Illuminated  
      - If AUW > 20460 lb (9300 kg) and/or H σ > 7200 ft,  
        NR + 10 ........................................... ON  
                                                            Check NR increases  
      - Bleed valves ...................................... Offset threshold  
   
        NOTE  : The bleed valve threshold offset is not mandatory when the      R   
               external conditions allow correct engine performance in hover   R   
               flight with bleed valves closed (head wind, no precipitation,   R   
               etc...).                                                        R   
   
      - Nose wheel ........................................ Locked  
      - Parking brake ..................................... Released  
      - Caution Advisory Panel lights ..................... All off  
      - Pressure and temperature readings ................. Correct  
      - Fuel transfer ..................................... Off  
      - Heating and demisting systems ..................... As required  
      - MPAI .............................................. As required  
                                                            . Refer to   
                                                              paragraph 8  
      - Passenger ordinance lights ........................ Illuminated  
   
12.2  Approach   
   
      All approach paths complying with the airspeed-height diagram and with  
      aircraft performance limitations are authorized.  
      
 
 532 A2 4.1
  
                   07-24 Page 20
 *01* 

PDF PAGE 343
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 4.1 
 
                        06-34 Page 21
 *01*
 
  
12.3  Recommended Approach Procedure (Refer to landing distances in Section 5 
                                      Performance Data). 
  
      - Disengage upper modes if currently engaged. 
      - Gradually reduce speed to begin final approach at a height of 50 ft 
        above the landing site, at an airspeed of 30 knots and a rate of 
        descent of about 300 ft/minute. 
      - Maintain an airspeed of about 30 knots until near ground level. 
      - Reduce airspeed to enter hover at a height of about 10 ft. 
      - Maintain hover IGE if appropriate. 
  
      NOTE : For Gross Weight up to maximum twin engine hover performance      
             IGE 10 ft, the same procedure apply but :                         
             - Maintain an airspeed of 40 kt instead of 30 kt                  
             - Do not try to maintain hover IGE.                               
             - Land with approximately 10 kt of airspeed.                      
  
12.4  Normal Landing 
  
      - Touch down vertically. 
  
        CAUTION : NOSE-UP ATTITUDE MUST NOT EXCEED 10° NEAR GROUND LEVEL TO 
                  PREVENT TAIL ROTOR GUARD FROM STRIKING THE GROUND. 
  
      - Cyclic stick ........................................ Centered          
      - Collective lever .................................... Low-pitch stop    
      - AFCS ................................................ Disengaged        
        . Recover manual control warning displayed in AFCS status zone. 
      - Nose wheels ......................................... Unlocked 
  
        CAUTION : ON GROUND, IN ORDER TO PREVENT ANY RISK OF VERTICAL           
                  OSCILLATIONS, THE TRIM LOADS MUST BE RELEASED BY DEPRESSING   
                  THE COLLECTIVE TRIM RELEASE TRIGGER WHENEVER THE COLLECTIVE   
                  PITCH IS MOVED.                                              R 
  
12.5  Running Landing 
  
      - Follow the recommended approach procedure until aircraft nears ground 
        level. 
      - Reduce airspeed to touch down at about 20 kt. 
  
        CAUTION : DO NOT APPLY WHEEL BRAKES UNTIL COLLECTIVE LEVER IS MOVED     
                  FULLY TO LOW-PITCH STOP. 
  
12.6  Landing on Sloping Ground                                                 
  
      The slope angle shall not exceed 12° with the aircraft headed up the     R 
      slope and 8° with the aircraft across the slope.                         R 
      - Parking brake ....................................... Applied 
      - Nose wheels ......................................... Locked 
      For stopping the rotor, the slope gradients shall not exceed : 
        . 5°  lateral                                                          R 
        . 10° longitudinal                                                     R 
      - Touch down point by point by gradually reducing the collective pitch   R 
        and using the cyclic stick to prevent the aircraft from rolling down   R 
        the slope.                                                             R 
  
 

PDF PAGE 344
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 4.1 
 
                        15-44 Page 22
 *01*
 
  
      - When all wheels are in contact with the ground, continue to reduce      
        the collective pitch gradually while simultaneously returning the       
        cyclic stick to neutral and monitoring the aircraft attitude.           
                                                                                
      CAUTION : 1. IF LANDING IS MADE ON ONE MAIN WHEEL, THEN LATERAL           
                   OSCILLATIONS MAY OCCUR; THESE WILL BE STOPPED BY TAKING      
                   OFF OR BY FIRM LANDING DEPENDING ON POSSIBILITIES.           
                                                                                
                2. IF CYCLIC STICK REACHES A TRAVEL LIMIT STOP, ABORT THE       
                   LANDING PROCEDURE.                                           
                                                                                
                3. ON UNEVEN GROUND, ESPECIALLY WITH AFT CG LOADING, TAKE       
                   CARE TO PREVENT THE TAIL FROM STRIKING THE GROUND.           
                                                                                
                4. WHEN HEADED UP THE SLOPE, THE AIRCRAFT MAY ROLL BACKWARD     
                   AS THE COLLECTIVE PITCH IS REDUCED DUE TO NO BRAKING         
                   SYSTEM ON THE NOSE WHEELS.                                   
  
12.7 Check after landing                                                       R 
 
     Refer to SUP.45 for aircraft equipped with ELECTRONIC WARFARE SYSTEM      R 
     (EWS).                                                                    R 
 
13  ENGINE SHUTDOWN 
  
    CAUTION : EXCEPT IN AN EMERGENCY, DO NOT USE THE FUEL SHUTOFF VALVE 
              CONTROLS TO SHUT DOWN THE ENGINES. 
  
    - Parking brake ...................................... Applied 
    - Nose wheel ......................................... Lock if necessary 
    - Cyclic stick ....................................... Neutral position 
    - Collective lever ................................... Secure in position 
    - Radiocommunication and navigation systems .......... Off 
    - FLIR ............................................... Off                 
      (Before switching off the FLIR, let it in standby mode applicable to     R 
      CLIO S FLIR or stowage mode applicable to STAR SAFIRE 380-HD, more than  R 
      30 s.                                                                    R 
    - Landing gear ....................................... Safety pin in place 
    - Pitot heating and windshield deicing systems ....... Off 
    - Allow engines to stabilize at minimum pitch setting (see NOTE):           
      . for 1 minute if OAT exceeds - 10°C. 
      . for 2 minutes if OAT is below - 10°C. 
    - MPAI ............................................... As required          
                                                           . Refer to           
                                                             paragraph 8        
    - Booster pumps ...................................... Off 
    - Fuel flow controls ................................. Shutdown position 
    - Rotor brake safety lever ........................... Aft position 
    - Rotor brake ........................................ Apply when NR is 
                                                           120 rpm (45 %) 
                                                           or less 
    - All switches ....................................... Off 
  
    NOTE   : Engine stabilization time prior to shutdown may include taxiing    
             time when aircraft is taxied at low power near low-pitch setting 
             with low relative wind velocity.

          cabin fuel tanks are not used.

2.3  Fuel Transfer Instructions
                                                  
   
     - Fuel may be transferred at any time except during takeoff and landing  
       procedures.  
     - Stop transferring fuel when fuel quantities are approximately the  
       same in both fuel tank groups.  
     - Interrupt the transfer procedure if the High Level light illuminates  
       for the receiving fuel tank group.  
     - Interrupt the transfer procedure if the Low Level light illuminates  
       for the source fuel tank group.  
       Check the amount of fuel remaining in the source feeder tank.  
       Resume transfer if the remaining fuel quantity exceeds 90 litres  
       (24 US gal, 20 UK gal, 71 kg, 157 lb).  
   
   
   
   
   
   
                                                                                
                                                                               R

2.4  Fuel Transfer to Correct Longitudinal CG Position
     a) Recommended procedure  
        - Begin fuel transfer after consuming approximately 150 litres  
          (40 US gal, 33 UK gal, 118 kg, 260 lb) of fuel from both fuel tank  
          groups (i.e. after about 1/2-hour of flight).  
        The aircraft CG position moves about 2 cm forward before transfer,  
        then aft about 4 cm during the transfer.  
     b) Fuel Transfer at Beginning of Flight : the aircraft CG position  
        moves about 2 cm aft and stabilizes.  
2.5  Untransferable Fuel
     - With booster pumps :  
       LH group : 50 l (13 US gal - 11 UK gal - 39 kg - 87 lb)  
       RH group : 10 l (2.64 US gal - 2.2 UK gal - 7.9 kg - 17.4 lb)             
     - Without booster pumps :  
       LH group :  90 l (24 US gal - 20 UK gal - 71 kg - 157 lb)  
       RH group : 190 l (50.2 US gal - 41.8 UK gal - 150.1 kg - 330.9 lb)        
3  FLIGHT OR PARKING AT HIGH NOSE-UP ATTITUDES
   High nose-up attitudes may be due to the individual or combined effects  
   of an aft CG position, low airspeed, high rate of climb, wind in hover  
   flight, sloping ground in parking area, etc.  
3.1  Consequences of High Nose-Up Attitudes for Extended Periods
     The possible consequences on fuel system operation include :  
     1. Illumination of the low level light for the LH tank group while the  
        remaining fuel quantity in this group is above 157 lb (90 litres).  
     2. Unusable fuel quantity in the LH group :  
        78 lb (45 litres).  
     3. Unusable fuel quantity in the RH group :  
        27.9 lb (16 litres).  
3.2  General Instructions
     1. Adopt a flight (or parking) configuration with a more level  
        attitude as soon as possible.  
     2. If fuel level is low in the LH tank group, fuel may be transferred  
        from the RH group to the LH group (refer to Fuel Transfer Instructions  
        in paragraph 2 above).  
                                                                              R

4  OPERATION IN HIGH WINDS  (Wind velocity : 30 kt or more)  
   The wind envelope for starting or stopping the rotor is specified             
   in Section 2.1.                                                               
4.1  Parking and Handling
     Park the aircraft into the wind within +  30°.  
4.2  Starting Procedures
     The standard starting procedure is modified as follows :  
     - Auxiliary hydraulic pump .................... On  
       . LH hydraulic system pressure .............. Normal  
     - Cyclic stick ................................ Centered  
     - Rotor brake ...................................Dynamic position  
      . RB ON light ..................................Illuminated  
     - Static braking inhibit lever ..................Raised                         
     - Rotor brake ...................................Static position.  
  PROCEDURE BEFORE THE FIRST FLIGHT OF THE DAY       
   |                                                                |  
   |- Rotor brake.................................Forward           |  
   |                                                                |  
   |- Static braking enable lever.................Lowered           |  
   |                                                                |  
   |- Rotor brake RB.ON light.....................Off               |  
   |                                                                |  
   |- Rotor brake.................................Dynamic position  |  
   |                                                                |  
   |- Rotor brake RB.ON light.....................Illuminated       |  
   |                                                                |  
   |- Static braking enable lever.................Raised            |  
   |                                                                |  
   |- Rotor brake.................................Static position   |  
   |                                                                |  
     - Start one engine ..............................Check that rotor does  
not spin  
      CAUTION  : IF ROTOR BEGINS SPINNING TOO EARLY  
                . BEFORE GROUND IDLE SPEED IS REACHED : SHUT DOWN THE ENGINE    
                . AFTER GROUND IDLE SPEED IS REACHED  : RELEASE ROTOR BRAKE     
                                                        AND ACCELERATE THE      
                                                        ENGINE.                 
    - After stabilization at ground idling speed, adjust Ng to 63-66 % as  
      required using the fuel flow control.  
    - Control the cyclic pitch with one hand.  
                                                                               R 
 532 A2 4.2
12-23 Page 9
*01*

PDF PAGE 356
FLIGHT MANUAL 
    CAUTION  : THE CYCLIC STICK MUST REMAIN CENTERED DURING THE ACCELERATION  
              PHASE.  
    - With the other hand, release the rotor brake to the full forward  
      position and move the fuel flow control to the flight detent  
      (Torque : approx 35 %).  
    - Check that static braking enable lever is lowered  
    - Caution Advisory Panel RB ON light..........Off  
    NOTE  : Only one engine may be running at no more than 70 % Ng with the  
           rotor brake applied for 5 minutes maximum.  
4.3  Shutdown Procedures
     - Auxiliary hydraulic pump....................On  
     - Proceed with normal shutdown.  
     - Immobilize the flight controls.  
5  OPERATION IN COLD WEATHER  (temperatures below -30°C)  
5.1  Before Starting
     - Carry out preliminary heating and cleaning operations.  
     - Whenever possible, use batterie that have been stored in a warm  
       place.  
5.2  Starting Procedure
     - Follow the standard engine starting procedure.  
     - Proceed with a 5-minute ground run at starting detent.                    
     - Switch on the cabin heating and demisting systems.  
     - Gradually increase power while monitoring the engine oil pressure.  
5.3  After Starting
     - Move flight controls over limited range.  
       . Check hydraulic pressure readings and trim release function.  
       . Switch off the AP hydraulic system and check for stiffness while  
         operating the flight controls. Continue heating if necessary until  
         flight controls operate smoothly.  
     - Perform the standard checks required after starting engines.  
     - If the OAT is below -35°C, manually center the nose landing gear  
       wheel or align it by taxiing for a few meters, in order to ensure  
       subsequent landing gear retraction.  
                                                                               R 
 532 A2 4.2
12-23 Page 10
*01*

FLIGHT MANUAL 
6  FLIGHT IN SEVERE TURBULENCE
   The flight parameters recommended in severe turbulence are as follows :  
   - Minimum airspeed : Vy  
   - Maximum airspeed : 135 kt (TAS)  
7  ILS APPROACH
7.1  Approach performed with a single VOR/ILS system
     When the aircraft is equipped with a single VOR/ILS system, check  
     that guiding redundancy is ensured by another system or with the  
     assistance of a ground radar ; adapt the minimum values accordingly.  
7.2  Use of NR ILS system
     If an ILS frequency is displayed, the blue NR ILS indicator light           
     illuminates on the instrument panel. A press on the pushbutton   
     extinguishes the light and allows NR to be reduced by 6 rpm approx.  
     (thus avoiding any possible oscillations of the LOC signal).  
8  OPERATION AT HIGH ALTITUDES  (higher than 13000 ft)  
   Recommendations for high-altitude flight :  
   - avoid sudden collective pitch variations,  
   - Restrict bank angles to 30°.  
9  OPERATION IN SAND-LADEN ATMOSPHERE
    In the following different flight phases and conditions :                    
    - Frequent landing and takeoff in sand-laden atmosphere,                     
    - Frequent hover IGE above a sandy ground,                                   
    The following configuration is recommended :                                 
    - Ventilation/heating 7-position selector .................  OFF             
    If required :                                                                
    - Ventilation/heating 7-position selector,                                   
      head/feet position ......................................  Selected        
    - Temperature setting knob ................................  As required     
 10  OPERATING DOORS IN FLIGHT                                                  R  
     Closing of lateral sliding doors must be performed by slow maneuver,      R  
     particularly at the end of the sliding motion.                            R  
                                                                               R

11  ENGINE POWER CHECK                                                          R  
11.1  Engine Power Check on the Ground                                          R  
      The purpose of the engine power check is to determine the available  
      power relative to the minimum guaranteed power.  
11.1.1  Checking Principles                                                    R  
        - The engine power is checked by comparison with reference data, by  
          entering the parameters recorded on the ground in Figure 1 then  
          in Figure 2 below.  
          NOTE  : If questionable or unstable results are obtained (notably due  
                 to wind interference) it is advisable to repeat the test  
                 procedure at different Ng values.  
        - If any result is unsatisfactory, proceed with an inflight power  
          check.  
11.1.2  Recording Conditions                                                   R  
        The check is to be performed with the MPAI bullets in open position.  
        - Head the aircraft into the wind on a clear area.  
        - Bleed valve offset disabled.  
        - After starting both engines, let them run at idling speed, then set  
          one engine to IDLE/TRAINING (this engine will remain at idling speed  
          even if the collective pitch lever is raised ; the other engine  
          automatically passes onto TRAINING mode).  
        - Disable the OEI HI rating.                                             
        - Switch off all P2 bleed air flow.  
        - Raise the collective pitch lever to obtain the maximum available  
          power (from the engine which is not in IDLE mode) at the limit of  
          liftoff, without allowing rotor speed to drop below 255 rpm (96 %).    
        - Disconnect AFCS in case of oscillations.  
        - Check that the bleed valve is closed and that parameter readings are  
          stable.  
        - Record the following values on the engine in TRAINING mode :  
          . engine torque           : Tq (%)  
          . gas generator speed     : Ng (%)  
          . rotor speed             : NR (rpm or %)  
          . pressure altitude       : Hp (ft)  
          . outside air temperature : OAT (°C)  
        - Apply the same procedure for checking the other engine.

11.1.3  Power Check (Figure 1 and Figures 2a, 2b)                              R  
        - The corrected Ng value is determinated on Figure 1 chart.  
        - The available power, represented by point A, is determined in the  
          lower portion of the Figures 2a, 2b charts.                          R  
        - The minimum guaranteed power (an engine removal criterion)  
          represented by point B is determined in the upper portion of the  
          same charts.                                                         R  
        - Evaluate as follows :  
          . If point A is to the LEFT of point B  : Engine condition is  
            SATISFACTORY . (Power margin is 1 % per graduation).  
          . If point A is to the RIGHT of point B  : Engine may be defective .  
            Proceed as indicated in 11.1.1 to confirm the engine condition.    R  
11.2  Check of Engine Power and Thermal Condition                               R  
      This in-flight check is intended to monitor the engine power and thermal  
      condition.  
11.2.1  Checking Principles                                                    R  
        - The engine power is checked by comparison with reference data, by  
          entering the parameters recorded in flight in Figures 1, 2a, 2b      R  
          and 3.                                                               R  
11.2.2  Recording Conditions                                                   R  
        - Shut off any P2 bleed air flow.  
        - Maintain stable flight for three minutes :  
          . in level flight at Max. Continuous Power AEO,  
          . at an altitude between 0 and 15000 ft.  
        - Record the following values :  
          . engine torque           : Tq (%)  
          . gas generator speed     : Ng (%)  
          . exhaust gas temperature : t4 (°C)  
          . rotor speed             : NR (rpm or %)  
          . pressure altitude       : Hp (ft)  
          . outside air temperature : OAT (°C)  
          . aircraft airspeed       : IAS (kt)     
 532 A2 4.2
12-23 Page 13
*01*

PDF PAGE 362
FLIGHT MANUAL 
11.2.3  Engine Power and Thermal Condition check                               R  
        - Determine the corrected value of the gas generator speed (Ng)  
          using Figure 1 and, from this value, compute the gross power  
          margin, using the charts of Figures 2a, 2b.                          R  
          The net margin is obtained by subtracting the following values  
          from the gross margin :  
                     2 % for IAS >  140 kt  
                     1 % for IAS = 130 kt  
                     0 % for IAS <  120 kt  
        Example :    Engine Torque                    Tq  = 38.5 %  
                     Outside Air Temperature          OAT = + 6°C  
                     Recorder gas generator speed     Ng  = 92.8 %  
                     Corrected gas generator speed    Ng  = 92.8 %  
                     Rotor speed                      NR  = 270 rpm (101.9 %)  
                     Pressure altitude                Hp  = 3000 ft  
                     Indicated Airspeed               IAS = 140 kt  
                     Gross margin : A - B = 3 %  
                     Net margin   : Gross margin - 2 % = 1 %  
        - Calculate the net t4 margin by making the following difference :  
                    t4 given by the chart of Figure 3 minus the t4 read.  
        - Check that the net margins (power and t4) are greater than or  
          equal to zero. If they are not, confirm these values by performing  
          a reference check (if the values are actually confirmed, refer to  
          the Engine Maintenance Manual).  
11.3  Reference Check                                                           R  
11.3.1  Checking principles                                                    R  
        - This check must be carried out if the result of the check is not  
          satisfactory.  
        - The purpose of this in-flight check is to determine the margin  
          of the engine at low altitude (at Hp <  5000 ft) and at higher power  
          level than during the check.  
11.3.2  Recording Conditions                                                   R  
        - Shut off any P2 bleed air flow.  
        - Maintain stable flight for three minutes :  
          . in level flight with the engine to be checked at Training OEI HI     
            power without allowing rotor speed to drop below 255 rpm (96 %),     
          . at an altitude between 0 and 5000 ft.  
        - Record the parameters specified in paragraph 11.2.2.                 R 
 532 A2 4.2
12-23 Page 14
*01*

PDF PAGE 363
FLIGHT MANUAL 
11.3.3  Calculation of the Reference Margin                                    R  
        Calculate the margin as per paragraph 11.2.3.                          R  
        Should the margin be equal to or less than zero, confirm the result  
        by repeating the procedure of paragraph 11.3.2.                        R  
        If the result is actually confirmed, refer to the Engine Maintenance  
        Manual.

*01*
            EFFECT OF AN OPTIONAL EQUIPMENT ITEM ON PERFORMANCE 
1  EFFECT ON TAKEOFF WEIGHT 
   - Determine the required performance in the Basic Flight Manual without 
     allowing for the maximum aircraft weight unit. 
   - Apply the weight penalty given by the supplement covering the optional 
     equipment. 
   - Compare with the maximum aircraft weight and use the smaller of the 
     two values. 
   Examples : An optional equipment item reducing the maximum takeoff weight 
              by 221 lb (100 kg). 
   Case 1 : 
   - Maximum aircraft takeoff 
     weight under certain conditions 
     of (Hp, OAT) : 21627 lb  
     (9810 kg) 
   - Performance with optional 
     equipment 21627 - 221 = 21406 lb 
     (9810 - 100 = 9710 kg) 
   - Max. weight = 21500 lb 
     (9750 kg) 
     Use the value of 21406 lb 
     (9710 kg) 
   Case 2 : 
   - Maximum aircraft takeoff 
     weight under certain conditions 
     of (Hp, OAT) : 21826 lb 
     (9900 kg) 
   - Performance with optional 
     equipment 21826 - 221 = 21605 lb 
     (9900 - 100 = 9800 kg) 
   - Max. weight = 21500 lb 
     (9750 kg) 
     Use the value of 21500 lb 
     (9750 kg) 

PDF PAGE 411
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.0 
 
                        97-50 Page 3
 *01*
 
  
2  EFFECT ON RATE OF CLIMB                                                      
  
   Apply the penalty on the Basic Manual performance. 
  
  
3  EFFECT ON LEVEL FLIGHT PERFORMANCE                                           
  
   Apply the penalty on the Basic Manual performance. 
  
  
4  SIMULTANEOUS USE OF OPTIONAL EQUIPMENT ITEMS - EFFECT ON PERFORMANCE         
  
   Add the penalties of each optional equipment item and apply the 
   preceding paragraphs.

                                                                               *01* 
                           LIST OF EFFECTIVE PAGES 
                       EFFECTIVITY (AIRCRAFT) FOR SRG 
(1) EFFECTIVITY: 
    - Without indication ...... Applicable to all aircraft. 
    - Indicated ............... Specific to indicated civilian airworthiness. 
    - Clear text definition ... Self-explanatory. 
(2) VARIANT OF STANDARD DEFINITION EFFECTIVITY: 
    - Without indication ...... Applicable to all aircraft. 
    - XXX ..................... Specific to aircraft equipped with XXX. 
(3) GROUP OF AIRCRAFT EFFECTIVITY: 
    - Without indication ...... Applicable to all aircraft. 
    - A........................ Specific to S/N 2469 - 2472 - 2473 - 2475 - 2478 
                                                2482 - 2491 - 2496 - 2502 - 2506 
                                                2518 - 2525. 
|                               |         |           |           |           | 
|   SECTION,          PAGE      |  DATE   |    (1)    |    (2)    |    (3)    | 
|   SUP or APP                  |  CODE   |           |           |           | 
|                               |         |           |           |           | 
|   SUP.3.P1            1*01*   |  14-21  |           |           |A          | 
|   SUP.3.P5         1/03*01*   |  14-21  |           |           |A          | 
|   SUP.3               1*01*   |  10-25  |           |           |A          | 
|   SUP.3               2*01*   |  98-33  |           |           |A          | 
|   SUP.3               3*01*   |  07-24  |           |           |A          | 
|   SUP.3               4*01*   |  10-25  |           |           |A          |

1  GENERAL 
1.1  Applicability 
     The information contained in this supplement covers the OEI flight 
     training procedures. 
     It supplements or replaces the information contained in category A        R 
     Supplement.                                                                
1.2  Principle 
     The maximum OEI ratings specified in section 2 cannot be used unless an 
     actual engine failure occurs. 
     A TRAINING system enables OEI flight training to be conducted using 
     non-damaging power levels, providing that the weights have been reduced 
     accordingly. 
     To this end, the system enables the following operations to be performed 
     simultaneously : 
     - Before MOD 07 26304 :                                                   
       . Simulation of an engine failure by reducing the power of that engine 
         to idle rating, with the Nf being governed at a value slightly lower 
         than 245 rpm (92.5 %) equivalent NR.                                     
     - After MOD 07 26304 :                                                    
       . Simulation of an engine failure by reducing the Ng to a fixed value   
         down or equal to the idle rating (Ng valued can change with OAT).     
     - derating the OEI stops of the other engine by approximately 5 % 
       (1665 rpm) in order to limit the Ng to a value not exceeding the 
       takeoff power rating. 
     Moreover, the system incorporates devices to allow automatic power        R 
     recovery of the idling engine in cases of incorrect training manoeuvre    R 
     or actual engine failure during the training session.                     R 
     Nevertheless, considering specific hazards associated to OEI training,    R 
     it is recommended to reduce the number of persons on board to what is     R 
     necessary for instruction.                                                R 

PDF PAGE 490
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.3
98-33 Page 2
*01*
1.3  Controls and Indicators 
     One guarded switch, associated to each engine, is provided on the 
     overhead control unit ; this switch is used to perform the following  
     actions simultaneously : 
     - to reduce power to TRAINING IDLE rating on the engine concerned, 
     - to derate the OEI limits of the Ng on the other engine. 

PDF PAGE 491
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.3 
 
                        07-24 Page 3
 *01*
 
  
     When an engine failure is simulated, the parameters displayed are similar 
     to those displayed in the event of actual engine failure, except for the 
     t4 torque, the minimum airspeed in level flight at the OEI LO rating and  R 
     the symbols associated with the TRAINING function. 
  
     - The DIFF NG light illuminates to indicate a mismatch between the Ng 
       values (information output by the helicopter POWER LOSS computer). 
  
     - The First Limit Indicator (FLI) switches to the OEI TRAINING mode 
       (data output by the DECU unit) and displays : 
       . the corresponding limitations, 
       . the OEI HI or OEI LO symbols, depending on the selection, 
       . the T symbol. 
  
     - The Δ NG indicator receives the derated limitations and is used as 
       in the case of an actual engine failure. It denotes the position of 
       the OEI stop. 
  
     - The PWR 1 and PWR 2 lights function as they would if the engine had 
       really failed. 
  
     - The Training warning lights T illuminate in the centre of the 
       instrument panel. 
  
     - The torque and t4 readings are farther from the limit values. 
  
     - The symbols associated with the operating limits and with the periods 
       of operation at the OEI ratings (in TRAINING mode) are displayed by 
       the IFDS, but the operating periods are not counted for the engine 
       maintenance purposes. 
  
     - The controls to derate the OEI HI and OEI LO stops are unchanged.         
   
     - Before MOD 07 26312:                                                    R  
       . The minimum airspeed in level flight at the OEI LO rating,              
         calculated and displayed by the IFDS, does not take into account  
         the power derating and is lower than it should be in all the cases.   R  
   
     - After MOD 07 26312:                                                     R  
       . The power derating is taken into account.                             R  
   
     CAUTION : THIS VALUE OF AIRSPEED AT THE OEI LO TRAINING RATING, DISPLAYED R  
               BY THE IFDS, IS INDICATIVE AND REMAINS LOWER THAN IT SHOULD BE. R  
               IT IS RECOMMENDED TO INCREASE IT BY APPROX. 2kt.                R  
 

PDF PAGE 492
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.3
10-25 Page 4
*01*
1.4  Function Safety Devices                                                    
     In addition to limiting the power to non-damaging ratings, the following 
     safety devices are provided when using the TRAINING function : 
     - In the event of an incorrect training manoeuvre or an actual failure of 
       the engine supplying the power, the required power can be obtained 
       from the idling engine simply by pulling the collective pitch lever. 
       The principle is as follows : as soon as the NR drops below 240 rpm, 
       (90.5 %) the idling engine supplies the amount of power required until 
       the actual OEI HI rating is reached (at NR = 220 rpm (83 %)),            
       using the static droop effect (see caution in chap . 3 of supplement 3).R 
     - There is no risk of a false manoeuvre with the fuel flow control 
       levers, since these levers remain in flight position ; the OEI is 
       simulated using specific controls. 
     - In the event of unintentional action on more than one TRAINING IDLE 
       control, the function is inhibited and a minor governing fault 
       is indicated. 
       The function will be reestablished after landing, shutting down both 
       engines and re-starting the engines according to the standard 
       procedure. 
     - Should the engine running in TRAINING IDLE mode fail, the procedure 
       can be continued at the actual OEI rating (i.e. with a larger power 
       margin) by setting the TRAINING IDLE control forward (switch guard 
       down). 
     - Return to twin-engine flight is possible at any time by setting the 
       TRAINING IDLE control forward (switch guard down). 
2  LIMITATIONS 
   The limitations given in the basic manual and its category A supplement 
   remain applicable with the exception of : 
   - Maximum takeoff weight on clear area ................... Figure 1a and 1b 
   - Maximum landing weight on clear area and on helipad .... Figure 2a and 2b 
   - Maximum takeoff weight on helipad ...................... Figure 3          
   NOTE : Maximum takeoff weight on helipad with MPAI bullets open is given in  
          Figure 3.                                                             
          With MPAI bullets closed, the data given in Figure 3 must be          
          reduced by 2 %.

3  EMERGENCY PROCEDURES 
   The emergency procedures given in the basic Flight Manual and its category 
   A supplement remain applicable during engine failure training sessions. 
   Normal operation can be obtained at any time by setting the TRAINING IDLE 
   switch forward (switch guard down). 
   Should the Training function be lost due to incorrect operation of the 
   TRAINING IDLE controls : 
   - Set both TRAINING IDLE switches........................... forward, 
                                                                switch guards 
                                                                down 
          LAND AS SOON AS PRACTICABLE 
   - After landing ............................................ shut down the 
                                                                engines 
   The OEI TRAINING function will be available after the engines have been 
   started according to the normal procedures. 
   CAUTION : IN CASE OF A REAL ENGINE FAILURE OF THE ENGINE SUPPLYING THE      R 
             POWER, THE NR MAY DROP VERY RAPIDLY, CONTROL COLLECTIVE IN        R 
             ORDER TO ADJUST NR BETWEEN 225 AND 235 RPM.                       R 
4  NORMAL PROCEDURES 
   After starting the engines, check operation of the TRAINING IDLE controls : 
   - Engine 1 TRAINING IDLE switch............................. backward 
     . Engine 1 Ng drops 
     Before MOD 07 26304 :                                                     
     . Engine 1 Nf is stabilized at approximately 240 rpm (90.5 %), 
       equivalent NR. 
     After MOD 07 26304 :                                                      
     . Engine 1 Nf can stay synchronized (depending on OAT value)

PDF PAGE 496
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.3 
 
                        99-20 Page 6
 *01*
 
  
   - Engine 2 fuel flow control lever.......................... fast reduction 
                                                                detent 
     . Engine 1 Ng increases slightly 
  
   - Engine 2 fuel flow control lever.......................... forward 
                                                                detent 
  
   - Engine 1 TRAINING IDLE switch............................. forward, 
                                                                switch guard 
                                                                down 
     . Engine 1 accelerates to come into line with Engine 2. 
  
   - Engine 2 TRAINING IDLE : same procedure as for Engine 1. 
  
   In flight, actuate the TRAINING IDLE control at the time appointed for the 
   failure. 
  
   Check that the training OEI HI rating (FLI and Δ NG) is obtained with       R 
   a 250-rpm (94 %) NR reading. 
  
   Reduce the power and set the TRAINING IDLE switch forward to return the 
   aRrcraft to normal twin-engine operation. 
  
  
   CAUTION : IF THE TRAINING IDLE SWITCH IS SET FORWARD WHEN THE ENGINE 
             IN TRAINING MODE IS AT THE TRAINING OEI HI OR OEI LO RATING,      R 
             THERE IS A DANGER THAT THE ACTUAL OEI RATINGS MIGHT BE USED. 
  
5  PERFORMANCE DATA 
  
   The performance data given in the basic manual and its category A 
   supplement remain applicable with the exception of : 
  
   - Maximum takeoff and landing weight on clear area, 
     depending on the V.TOSS selected : 
     . with MPAI bullets open ........................... Figures 4a to 8a 
     . with MPAI bullets closed ......................... Figures 4b to 8b 
  
   - Climbing performance : 
     . with MPAI bullets open ........................... Figures 10a to 12a 
     . with MPAI bullets closed ......................... Figures 10b to 12b 
     To use these figures : 
     . first determine the index weight from Figure 9, then the rate of 
       climb (R/C) and the slope from Figures 10 to 12 (a or b). 
  
   NOTE : The takeoff and landing distances given in the category A            R 
          supplement remain applicable during the engine failure training 
          sessions in the TRAINING mode.

- a verticalspeedbetween+/-200ft/min,
- an lAS> Vy,
- a lateralspeedonengagementwithoutNAVmodebetween+/-10kt

1  GENERAL (Figure 1) 
  
   The installation includes : 
  
   - an AIR EQUIPEMENT variable speed hydraulic hoist (5), 
   - a hoist arm (4) secured to the outside of the aircraft, 
   - hydraulic power supply systems, 
   - electric power supply systems, 
   - hoisting controls, 
   - safety devices. 
  
1.1  Hoist (5) 
  
     The AIR EQUIPEMENT variable speed hoist is supplied from the LH 
     hydraulic power system and groups all driving and safety devices under 
     a housing assembly. 
  
     a) General characteristics of the hoist : 
  
        - operating load            600 lb       (272 kg) 
        - usable cable length       246 ft       (75 m) 
        - cable diameter            3/16 in      (4.5 mm) 
        - lowering speed            0 to 3 ft/s  (0.9 m/s) 
        - hoisting speed            0 to 3 ft/s  (0.9 m/s) 
  
     b) Operating characteristics : 
  
        - The cable speed can be varied as required. 
        - Speed variation is progressive, irrespective of the load. 
        - Pre-determined slowing-down is obtained between 6.5 ft + 1 ft 
          (2 meters ± .3 m) and the end-of-travel, during both upward and 
          downward travel. 
        - Stopping occurs automatically at the end of the upward or downward 
          travel sequence. 
  
          NOTE : The solenoid valve is cut off automatically after a 2 second 
                 time delay when no further UPWARD or DOWNWARD order is given. 
  
        - It is also possible to release the load instantaneously by cutting 
          the cable using a squib. 
  
1.2  Hoist Arm (4) 
  
     The hoist arm is located forward of the aircraft RH door. It consists of : 
     - a hoist support plate, 
     - a brace strut, 
     - a drag bar. 
  
     On the ground, the brace strut is placed in its intermediate position in 
     order to open the RH engine cowling. 
 

PDF PAGE 542
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.11 
 
                        99-04 Page 2
 *01*
 
  
1.3  Hydraulic Power Supply 
  
     The hoist is supplied from the helicopter LH hydraulic system. 
  
1.4  Electric Power Supply 
  
     The electric power supply to the UP/DOWN control and to the cable 
     cutter squib is ensured by two separate 28 Vdc systems, each protected 
     by 2 circuit-breakers (The circuit-breakers location is specified in 
     section 7.2). 
  
1.5  Hoist Installation Control and Monitoring (Figure 1) 
  
     The control and monitoring system includes : 
  
1.5.1  Mission Selector Switch (1) 
  
       The mission selector switch is located on the cockpit overhead panel. 
       When set to HOIST : 
  
       - It illuminates the green HOIST CONTROL AUTHORIZATION light located at R 
         the hoist operator's station. 
  
       - It enables control of the hoist up/down solenoid valve using the 
         hoist operator's handgrip. 
  
       - It supplies power to the emergency release electric circuit. 
  
       When the selector is set to OFF, hoist operation is inhibited, which 
       constitutes a safety measure. 
  
1.5.2  Hoist Operator's Handgrip (11)                                          R 
  
       The hoist operator's handgrip is stowed on the RH cabin door jamb and 
       includes : 
  
       - A stop pushbutton (9) which cuts off the solenoid valve.              R 
  
       - A thumbwheel (10) for hoist controlling UP/DOWN travel speed control. R 
  
1.5.3  Indicator Light (7)                                                     R 
  
       A green HOIST CONTROL AUTHORIZATION indicator light, located next to    R 
       the hoist operator, illuminates when the mission selector is set to the 
       HOIST position. 
 

PDF PAGE 543
FLIGHT MANUAL

1.6  Emergency Release (14)                                                     
     - EMERGENCY RELEASE pushbutton on pilot's and copilot's collective 
       lever grips (13) are used to fire the hoist squib, which cuts the        
       cable. 
     - If necessary, the hoist operator can cut the cable using hand shears 
       (12) located below the window forward of the RH cabin door.              
1.7  Safety Belt (2) 
     A safety belt, to be used by the hoist operator, is attached to the 
     structure by a metal cable. 
1.8  Sound Connection (3) 
     The hoist operator is in permanent ICS contact with the pilots. 
2  LIMITATIONS 
   The limitations specified in Section 2 of the basic Flight Manual remain 
   applicable, irrespective of the special limitations given below. 
   CAUTION : WHEN THE HOIST IS SUPPLIED WITH HYDRAULIC FLUID AIR 3520 (OR 
             MIL.H.5606) ITS OPERATING ENVELOPE IS LIMITED TO 40°C. 
2.1  Lateral Center of gravity limits                                          R 
     The datum is the aircraft plane of symmetry.                              R 
2.2  Maximum Permissible Load on the Hoist                                     R 
     - 600 lb (272 kg).                                                        R 

PDF PAGE 545
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.11 
 
                        15-44 Page 5
 *01*
 
  
2.3  Minimum Crew                                                               
  
     For hoist mission, an experienced hoist operator must be included in 
     the crew. 
  
     NOTE : If the presence of a hoist operator assistant is required for the 
            mission, the assistant shall wear a safety belt.  
  
2.4  Maximum Airspeed                                                           
  
     - When the hoist is not operated: (cable reeled-in, and without load).    R 
       . The VNE specified in the Basic Flight Manual remains unchanged        R 
         (Refer to SECTION 2, paragraph 9).                                    R 
                                                                               R 
     - When the hoist is operated:                                             R 
       . For operating the doors and flight with the doors open, apply         R 
         the limitations given in SECTION 2 paragraph 9.3.                     R 
                                                                               R 
       CAUTION 1: LIMIT AIRSPEED ACCORDING TO THE LOAD (SHAPE, WEIGHT).        R 
                                                                               R 
       CAUTION 2: THE HOIST OPERATOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR MONITORING THE LOAD.   R 
                  HE MUST KEEP THE TOTAL CABLE LENGTH AND THE HOOK IN VIEW     R 
                  PERMANENTLY.                                                 R 
 
2.5  Manoeuvring Limits                                                         
  
     Maximum bank angle in turn with cable unreeled, with or without            
     suspended load : 25°. 
  
2.6  Indicating Plate                                                           
  
     An Indicating plate bonded on the hoist arm reads : 
     MAX.LOAD : 600 lb (272 kg). 
  
2.7  Manual Cable Cutter                                                        
  
    Shears must be provided on board. 
  
3  EMERGENCY PROCEDURES 
  
   The emergency procedures given in Section 3 of the basic Flight Manual 
   remain applicable irrespective of the special emergency procedures given 
   below. 
  
3.1  Cable Cutting 
  
     - Actuate one of the pushbuttons located on the pilot's and copilot's 
       collective pitch levers. 
  
       NOTE : The cable cutter cannot be actuated unless the protection 
              circuit-breakers are set and the mission selector is set to 
              HOIST. 
  
     - In case of complete electrical failure, order the cable to be cut with 
       the hand shears which are at the hoist operator's disposal. 
 
 
 
 
 

PDF PAGE 546
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.11 
 
                        14-24 Page 6
 *01*
 
  
4  NORMAL PROCEDURES 
  
   The normal procedures given in Section 4 of the basic Flight Manual remain 
   applicable irrespective of the special procedures given below. 
 
4.1  Before Flight 
  
     EXTERNAL CHECKS:                                                          R 
 
     - Ensure that the hoist hook is placed in the UP position.                R 
  
     CABIN INTERNAL CHECKS:                                                    R 
 
     - Check the presence of manual cable cutter on board the aircraft.        R 
  
     CHECKS BEFORE STARTING ENGINES:                                           R 
 
     - Checks that the internal load or the fuel distribution (with external   R 
       tanks installed) allows the hoisting mission to be carried out within   R 
       the CG limits.                                                          R 
 
     CHECKS AFTER STARTING THE ENGINES, ONLY BEFORE HOISTING MISSION:          R 
 
     - Ensure that the hoist operates correctly in the upward and downward     R 
       travel.                                                                 R 
                                                                
     - Check condition of the cable, where crimped to the bottom cup of the    R 
       hook assembly.                                                          R 
                                                   
     - Ensure that the hoist hook is placed in the UP position.                R 
                                                                         
 
4.2  In Flight 
  
4.2.1  Before Hoisting 
  
       PILOT ACTIONS 
  
       - Check that the four hoist circuit-breakers are effectively engaged. 
       - Set the mission selector switch on the overhead panel to HOIST 
         position. 
         Moving the switch to this position enables : 
         . the UP/DOWN control of the hoist using the hoist operator's grip 
           (power supply of the electrical circuit is confirmed by             
           illumination of the green hoisting authorized indicator light 
           located in the cabin); 
         . the hoist cable to be cut using the pilot's or copilot's emergency 
           release pushbutton (on collective lever grip). 
                                                                                   
 
 
 
 
 
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                               
                                                                                
                                                                               R
 

PDF PAGE 547
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.11 
 
                        15-44 Page 7
 *01*
 
       OPERATOR ACTIONS 
  
       - Make sure that the safety belt is correctly attached and fasten the 
         belt. 
       - Check for correct electrical connection of the hoist operator's grip 
         and for correct ICS connection with the crew. 
       - After pilot's accord open the RH sliding door until locked position 
         is reached. 
       - Put on a glove to guide the cable. 
  
4.2.2  Hoisting 
 
       WARNING: TO AVOID ANY ELECTRIC SHOCK TO PERSONS (IN CONTACT WITH ANY    R 
                PART OF THE EXTERNAL INSTALLATION), A GROUND ELECTRICAL        R 
                CONNECTION IS NECESSARY BEFORE THE EXTERNAL INSTALLATION IS    R 
                TOUCHED BY THE GROUND OPERATOR. IF AN ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE  R 
                DEVICE SUCH AS A CABLE IS USED, IT HAS TO BE REMOVED AFTER THE R 
                HOISTING PHASE.                                                R 
  
       PILOT ACTIONS 
  
       - If a rescue is being performed, do not initiate forward flight 
         before the person is hoisted on board. 
       - With a suspended load, carry out only smooth manoeuvres, with slow 
         accelerations and decelerations; perform progressive turns. 
  
       OPERATOR ACTIONS 
  
       - When the green hoisting authorized indicator light comes on, 
         actuate the hoist operator's UP/DOWN control thumbwheel. 
      
       - Take all necessary precautions to prevent the load being hoisted 
         from striking the helicopter. 
  
                                                                               R 
 
4.2.3  After Hoisting 
  
       OPERATOR ACTIONS 
  
       - The hoist hook must be placed in the UP position at the end  
         of the hoisting operation and maintained in this position 
         throughout cruise flight. 
  
       PILOT ACTION 
  
       - Set the mission selector switch on the overhead panel to OFF. 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
                                                                                
                                                                                
 
 
                                                                               R
 

PDF PAGE 548
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.11
15-44 Page 8
*01* 
4.3  Hoist Operation on an Aircraft Fitted with the Gun-Rocket Launcher         
     Weapon System                                                              
     - Mixed weapons                                                            
       It is recommended that the rocket launcher be installed on the RH side.  
     - RH gun mounted on the RH side                                            
       It is recommended :                                                      
       . that only one peron at a time be lowered or raised.                    
       . that hoisting operations not be performed in winds exceeding 15 kt,    
         in order to minimize the risk of contact between the hoist cable and   
         the gun pod fairing.                                                   
     NOTE : In case of necessity, selective jettison of the RH gun is still     
            possible.

          or cable unreeled by more than 13 ft (4 m) with a minimum load of 
          66 lb (30 kg) (Demonstrated performance with a compact load).

1  GENERAL 
  
   The auxiliary power unit can be used to supply the electrical power         R 
   required by the different equipment, and via a P2 air bleed to condition 
   the air in the cockpit and cabin. 
  
   The APU alternator is coupled to the LH and RH three-phase power systems. 
  
   The warm air supplied by the APU is distributed using the basic heating 
   installation ducts via a connection located before the P2 valve. 
  
1.1  Description 
  
     The APU installation includes : 
  
     - On the transmission deck, under the sliding cowling, on the RH side, 
       aft of the MGB : 
  
      . the APU, 
      . the alternator, 
      . the fire detection and extinguishing systems. 
  
     - In the electrical cabinet : 
  
       . the fire monitoring unit, 
       . the fuel governor unit. 
  
     - On the console, the instrument panel and the overhead panel : 
  
      . the APU controls and indicators.

1.2  Turbine Control and Monitoring (Fig. 1) 
  
     (1) APU caption on the caution advisory panel 
  
         Color                      : yellow 
         Condition for illumination : switch (2) set to ARM or P2 FAIL 
                                      light on. 
  
     (2) OFF/ARM switch 
  
         Function                   : switches the installation on 
  
     (3) OFF/RESET pushbutton 
  
         Functions                  : . switches the APU off 
                                      . APU RESET 
  
     (4) START pushbutton/indicator light 
  
         S 
  
         Color                      : green 
         Function                   : comes on when the pushbutton is pressed 
                                      and remains on throughout the starting 
                                      sequence 
  
         ON 
  
         Color                      : green 
         Function                   : comes on when the APU reaches its 
                                      nominal rating 
  
     (5) FUEL VALVE light 
  
         OPEN 
  
         Color                      : green 
         Condition for illumination : opening of the fuel solenoid valve 
  
         FAIL 
  
         Color                      : yellow 
         Condition for illumination : opening of the fuel solenoid valve with 
                                      switch (2) set to OFF. (The APU light 
                                      on the caution advisory panel and the 
                                      WARN lights on the instrument panel 
                                      remain off).

1.3  P2 Control and Monitoring (Fig. 2) 
  
     (1) P2 VALVE pushbutton/indicator light 
  
         OPEN 
  
         Color                      : green 
         Condition for illumination : opening of the APU P2 valve 
         Functions                  : - monitors the opening of the P2 valve  
                                        when the heating/ventilation control 
                                        is operated, 
                                      - when pressed, reopens the P2 valve 
                                        when the basic installation 3-way  
                                        valve is already open. 
         FAIL 
  
         Color                      : yellow 
         Condition for illumination : opening of the APU P2 solenoid valve 
                                      with the ARM/OFF switch set to OFF. 
  
1.4  Fire Detection Control and Monitoring (Fig. 2) 
  
     (2) APU FIRE caption on the caution advisory panel 
  
         Color                      : red 
         Condition for illumination : if the SHOT light (3) comes on. 
  
     (3) SHOT percussion button/indicator light 
  
         Color                      : red 
         Condition for illumination : opening of a fire detector 
         Functions                  : - fire alarm, 
                                      - discharges the fire extinguisher when 
                                        pressed. 
  
     (4) FIRE D caption 
  
         Color                      : yellow 
         Condition for illumination : break in the fire detection circuit. 
  
     (5) 3-position switch 
         Used to test the unit logic circuits. 
         - FIRE SIMU (unstable)     : simulates a fire 
         - LINE TEST (unstable)     : simulates a break in the detection 
                                      circuit. 
  
1.5  Control and Monitoring of APU Coupling to the Main System (Fig. 3) 
  
     (1) APU pushbutton/indicator light 
  
         Color                      : blue/white 
         Condition for illumination : alternator voltage and frequency correct 
         Function                   : when pressed, couples the alternator 
                                      to the 3-phase system, 
                                      when coupling is complete, the white  
                                      light goes out ; the blue light remains 
                                      on.

1.6  Protection 
  
     The governor unit constantly monitors turbine operation and cuts the fuel 
     supply in the event of : 
  
     - overspeed, 
     - overheating, 
     - speed sensor failure, 
     - ignition fault, 
     - speed below the nominal rating, 
     - oil pressure fault. 
  
1.7  Fire Detection and Extinguishing (Fig. 4) 
  
     - A detection unit constantly monitors for line breaks and opening of a 
       fire detector. 
  
     - A line break causes the FIRE D light on the control unit to come on. 
  
     - The opening of a detector causes : 
       . APU FIRE caption to come on, 
       . SHOT pushbutton/light to come on, 
       . APU shut-down by cutting off the electrical power supply and closing 
         the fuel supply solenoid valve. 
  
     NOTE : The extinguisher bottle must be fired manually.

2  LIMITATIONS 
  
   The limitations given in the basic Flight Manual and relevant Supplements 
   remain applicable, but are completed by the following : 
  
2.1  Operating Limits 
  
     - On ground   : . temperature : -50°C to ISA +40°C                        R 
                     . terrain altitude from -1640 ft (-500 m) to 20000 ft     R 
                       (6100 m)                                                R 
                      
     - In flight   : . temperature : -45°C to ISA + 40°C                       R 
                     . after failure of both alternators                        
                     . pressure altitude < 13100 ft (4000 m)                    
                     . airspeed, except for the landing phases :               R 
                       70 kt < IAS < 130 kt                                    R 
                     . vertical speed > -1500 ft/min                             
  
2.2  Approved Lubricants 
  
     - NATO  0.150 
     - NATO  0.148 
     - MIL-H-23699 
  
2.3  Approved Fuels 
  
     - F34 
     - F35 
     - F43                                                                     R 
     - F44 
  
3  EMERGENCY PROCEDURES 
  
   The emergency procedures specified in the basic Flight Manual and in 
   Supplements remain applicable but are completed by the following : 
  
3.1  In Flight, Opening of the P2 Valve With APU Off 
  
     - Symptoms    : - Illumination of the yellow APU caption on the caution 
                       advisory panel. 
                     - Illumination of the yellow FAIL/P2 VALVE light on the 
                       control unit. 
  
     - Consequence : P2 air may flow from the engines to the APU. 
  
     - Procedure   : Press the P2 VALVE pushbutton/light. 
  
3.2  In Flight, Opening of the Fuel Solenoid Valve With APU Off 
  
     - Symptom      : Illumination of the yellow FAIL/FUEL VALVE on the APU 
                      control unit. 
  
     - Procedure    : Check that the APU OFF/ARM switch is set to OFF. 
  
  
                                 CONTINUE FLIGHT 
  
  
 

PDF PAGE 563
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.13 
 
                        15-44 Page 9
 *01*
 
  
3.3  In-flight Operation of the APU After Failure of Both Alternators           
  
     After failure of both alternators ALT1 and ALT2, the APU may be operated   
     in flight.                                                                 
  
  
     CAUTION : IT'IS RECOMMENDED THAT TRAINING FOR APU OPERATION BE PERFORMED   
               ON GROUND ONLY.                                                  
  
     - APU starting conditions :                                                
       . IAS = Vy                                                               
       . Cockpit and cabin air-conditioning.....................   OFF          
         Cooling pushbutton lights..............................   OFF          
         (For the rest of the flight)  
       . Downlink (For aircraft subject to mid-Life upgrade).....  OFF         R 
  
     - Comply with the procedure given in paragraph 4 of this Supplement.       
  
                                    CONTINUE FLIGHT                             
  
     To continue to fly with APU operating :                                    
  
     - In flight                                                                
       Perform gentle manoeuvres, slow accelerations and decelerations.         
  
     - Landing                                                                  
       Land in compliance with the normal procedures given in the Basic Flight  
       Manual.                                                                  
  
3.4  APU Running, Break in Fire Detector Lines                                  
  
     - Symptom      : Illumination of the yellow FIRE D light on the APU        
                      control unit. 
  
     - Procedure    : Set the APU OFF/ARM switch to OFF. 
  
3.5  APU Running, Opening of a Fire Detector                                    
  
     - Symptoms     : . Illumination of the red APU FIRE caption on the 
                        caution advisory panel. 
                      . Illumination of the red SHOT light on the APU control   
                        unit.      
  
     - Consequences : . The APU shuts down automatically. 
                      . The extinguisher firing circuit is armed. 
  
     - Procedure    : . Press the SHOT pushbutton on the APU control unit.

4  NORMAL PROCEDURES 
  
   The normal procedures specified in the basic Flight Manual and relevant 
   Supplements remain applicable but are completed by the following : 
  
4.1  APU Starting 
  
     CAUTION : THE APU MUST BE STARTED ONLY ON THE AIRCRAFT BATTERY.           R 
  
     - With BAT switch on the overhead panel set to ON. 
     - Before starting of the APU, the following systems must be set 
       to OFF, to avoid overloading the alternator : 
       . RH windscreen deicing             . LH windscreen deicing 
       . RH Pitot static press             . LH Pitot static press 
         anti-icing                          anti-icing 
       . RH Pitot total press anti-icing   . LH Pitot total press anti-icing 
       . RH engine Multipurpose Air Intake . LH engine Multipurpose Air Intake 
       . Windscreen wipers                 . Weather radar. 
     - Set the APU ARM/OFF to ARM ; the FUEL VALVE OPEN caption on the APU 
       control unit and the APU caption on the caution advisory panel come 
       on. 
     - APU Fire Protection System .............................  Fire and line 
                                                                 test 
       . LINE TEST : FIRE D Light .............................  lit.           
       . FIRE SIMU : SHOT + APU FIRE Warning Lights ...........  lit.           
       . APU ARM/OFF selector .................................  OFF 
       . OFF/RESET Pushbutton .................................  PRESS 
       . APU ARM/OFF selector .................................  ARM 
     - Select on both the RH fuel booster pumps on the fuel management 
       panel. 
     - Press the START pushbutton/light ; the S caption comes on until the 
       starting sequence is complete. 
  
       NOTE : - The starting sequence is automatic and is controlled by 
                the governor unit. 
  
              - If the APU cannot be started : 
                . before resetting the APU, 
                  APU ARM/OFF switch ..........................  OFF 
                . reset the APU by pressing the OFF/RESET pushbutton, 
                  then APU ARM/OFF switch .....................  ARM 
  
     - The ON caption on the START pushbutton/light comes on when the APU 
       reaches its nominal rating.
