4.2  Coupling to the Main System 
  
     When the APU has reached its nominal rating and the voltage output by 
     the alternator is correct (frequency/voltage), the blue and white lights 
     in the APU pushbutton on the power supply panel come on. 
     Pressing this button couples the alternator to the 3-phase systems and 
     causes the white light to go out. The yellow ALT 1 and ALT 2 lights 
     remain on. 
  
     Before selecting the APU alternator : 
     . Cockpit and cabin air conditioning......................  OFF 
     . Cooling pushbutton lights...............................  OFF 
  
     NOTE : Should the air conditioning system be operated on the ground,       
            under APU power, the following systems must be set to OFF :         
  
            - RH windscreen deicing            - LH windscreen deicing          
            - Control windscreen deicing       - LH Pitot static press          
            - RH Pitot static press              anti-icing                     
              anti-icing                       - LH Pitot total press           
            - RH Pitot total press               anti-icing                     
              anti-icing                       - LH engine Multipurpose         
            - RH engine Multipurpose Air         Air Intake                     
              Intake                           - Central windscreen wiper       
            - Main Windscreen wipers           - LH engine booster pumps        
            - One RH engine booster pump       - Weapon system                  
            - Weather radar                    - CHLIO-S FLIR or STAR SAFIRE   R 
            - Spectrolab Searchlight             380-HD                        R 
            - Equipment connected to cockpit   - Sand filter configuration of  R 
              sanitary plugs                     the MPAI system               R 
            - Center windshield deicing        - IFDS fan                      R 
      
 
            For aircraft subject to mid-life upgrade, set to OFF the           R 
            following equipment :                                              R 
            - Downlink                                                         R 
            - Barco mission display unit                                       R 
            - Video Recorder                                                   R 
  
4.3  P2 System Control 
  
     With the engines stopped, actuate either of the cabin and cockpit 
     heating controls on the heating/ventilation control panel. 
     - the 3-way P2 solenoid valve of the basic installation, and the APU P2 
       solenoid valve open. 
  
     NOTE : When the engines are shut down with the basic installation P2 
            solenoid valve open, the APU P2 solenoid valve can only be opened 
            by pressing the P2 VALVE pushbutton/light. 
  
4.4  Disengaged the Main System 
  
     Before disengaging the APU alternator : 
     - Cockpit and cabin air-conditioning......................   OFF 
       . Cooling pushbutton lights.............................   OFF 
 
 

PDF PAGE 566
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.13 
 
                        00-12 Page 12
 *01*
 
  
4.5  Switching Off the APU 
  
     The APU can be switched off automatically or manually. 
  
     - Automatically : 
       . if a fault is detected by the governor unit or if a fire condition is 
         detected. 
  
     - Manually : 
       by pressing the OFF/RESET pushbutton : 
       . the indicator lights go out ; the APU caption on the caution 
         advisory panel remains on. 
       Electrical locking is obtained by setting the ARM/OFF switch to OFF. 
       . the APU caption on the caution advisory panel goes out. 
  
4.6  Before Starting Engines 
  
     The Checks Before Starting Engines given in the basic Flight Manual 
     must be completed as follows : 
  
4.6.1  Starting the engines without using the APU 
  
       - APU ARM/OFF switch ..........................   OFF 
  
4.6.2  Starting the engines using the APU 
  
       With APU running, on the electrical power control panel : 
       - APU pushbutton/light ........................   coupled 
         . white light................................   OFF  
         . blue light.................................   ON  
       - Cockpit and cabin air-conditioning...........   OFF                   R 
       . Cooling pushbutton lights...................... OFF                   R 
  
4.7  After Starting Engines 
  
     If the engines have been started using the APU, the Checks After 
     Starting Engines given in the basic Flight Manual must be completed 
     as follows : 
  
     - APU OFF/RESET pushbutton/light ................   Press 
       . control unit lights .........................   OFF 
  
     - ARM/OFF switch ................................   OFF 
       . caution advisory panel APU caption ..........   OFF 
  
     - Cockpit and cabin air-conditioning.............   ON                    R 
       . Cooling pushbutton lights....................   ON                    R 
  
  
5  PERFORMANCE DATA 
  
   The performance data specified in basic Flight Manual and relevant 
   Supplements remain applicable.

EFFECTIVITY (AIRCRAFT) FOR SRG AL FAHD 
 
(1) EFFECTIVITY: 
 
    - Without indication ...... Applicable to all aircraft. 
    - Indicated ............... Specific to indicated civilian airworthiness. 
    - Clear text definition ... Self-explanatory. 
 
(2) VARIANT OF STANDARD DEFINITION EFFECTIVITY: 
 
    - Without indication ...... Applicable to all aircraft. 
    - XXX ..................... Specific to aircraft equipped with XXX. 
 
(3) GROUP OF AIRCRAFT EFFECTIVITY: 
 
    - Without indication ...... Applicable to all aircraft. 
    - A ....................... Specific to S/N 2469-2472-2473-2475-2478-2482

1  GENERAL 
  
   The installation includes : 
   - A release hook. 
   - A mechanical hooking assembly. 
   - An electrical control and monitoring system. 
   - A stand-by mechanical control. 
   - A placard (on RH side of the cockpit console).                            R 
  
1.1  Release hook 
  
     The release hook is capable of transporting a maximum load of 8360 lb      
     (3800 kg). As it is of swivel type, it leaves the load free to rotate. 
     It is fitted with : 
     - An electrical control (release and resetting). 
     - A mechanical control (release and resetting). 
  
1.2  Mechanical hooking assembly 
  
     A mechanical hooking assembly beneath the fuselage ensures the mechanical 
     coupling between the release hook and the airframe and enables the load 
     to oscillate to a cone with a 60° top angle. 
     A bungee cord is provided to retract the release hook aft when it is 
     not loaded. 
  
1.3  Electric control and monitoring system (Figure 1) 
  
     This system includes : 
     - A mission selector. 
     - The pilot's and copilot's electric release controls. 
     - An interface unit. 
     - A load indication displayed on the NMD screens. 
     - A load sensor. 
     - Electrical coupling circuits. 
  
1.3.1  Mission selector 
  
       The mission selector, which is located on the cockpit overhead 
       panel, is accessible to both pilots, and energizes the system when 
       set to SLING. 
       When set to OFF this switch inhibits the electric load jettison 
       function. 
  
1.3.2  Electric jettison controls 
  
       The pilot and copilot are both provided with a normal jettison control 
       on their cyclic stick grips. This control is marked CARGO and is 
       protected by a cup shaped knob which acts as a switch guard. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
 

PDF PAGE 582
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.15 
 
                        16-01 Page 2
 *01*
 
  
1.3.3  Load indication and indicating system 
  
       The load and system status indications are displayed on the NMDs.  
       Two display modes are available : 
       - Mission selector set to SLING :                                        
         The sling information is displayed whatever selection is made from     
         the DCU, except for the aircraft weight selection which has priority. 
       - Mission selector set to OFF :                                          
         The sling information is displayed by selecting the SLING function  
         using the DATA key on the DCU. 
  
1.3.4  Interface unit 
  
       The interface unit displays the information from the load sensor on  
       the NMDs. 
                                                                               R 
 
1.3.5  Load sensor 
  
       The load sensor is incorporated in the release hook.  
  
1.4  Stand-by mechanical control 
  
     A trigger secured to the pilot's collective lever grip opens the hook in 
     emergency mode via a setting box. 
  
     NOTE : When the stand-by control is used, the load release hook remains 
            open and can only be locked if the setting box has been reset.

  3.  Stand-by mechanical release          or 
      control                              hook not closed after release  
  4.  No sling                             (malfunction)

2  LIMITATIONS 
   Apart from the specific limitations given below, the limitation given in 
   Section 2 of the basic Flight Manual remain applicable. 
2.1  Underslung Load                                                            
     . Maximum permissible load : 8360 lb (3800 kg)                             
     . Minimum load on hook required for load release : 28.6 lb (13 kg)         
2.2  Maximum Weight 
     The maximum weight of the aircraft for takeoff and landing, with a 
     jettisonable underslung load, is the weight allowing hover flight 
     OGE on two engines. 
     - Absolute maximum weight : 23142 lb (10500 kg).                          
     - Modifications to the weight as a function of altitude and temperature 
       are given in figure 2 of the present Supplement. 
       FOR REFERENCE : Maximum weight of the aircraft, with the external load 
                       not included : 
                       . 21500 lb (9750 kg). 
2.3  Longitudinal Center-of-Gravity Limits 
                                Figure 2                                   R 
2.4  Maximum Speed 
     Maximum airspeed with compact underslung loads : 130 kt 
     CAUTION : IF THE UNDERSLUNG LOAD IS BULKY, DECREASE SPEED ACCORDINGLY.

 532 A2 SUP.15 
 
                        11-27 Page 5
 *01*
 
  
2.5  Maneuvering limitations                                                    
  
     Maximum bank angle with underslung load: 30 degrees. 
  
2.6  Use limitations                                                             
   
     The class of approved aircraft/load combination is : B "Single Point        
     Suspension External Load Airborne" meaning carriage of external loads       
     which are jettisonable and lifted free of land or water during the          
     rotorcraft operation.                                                       
   
     The external loads are limited to non-human loads only.

3  EMERGENCY PROCEDURE 
   Independently from the special procedures provided below, the emergency      
   procedures provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its supplements remain    
   applicable.                                                                  
3.1  Engine Failure with Underslung Load 
     Should an engine failure occur, either release the load or land with the 
     load, depending on performance and environment. 
3.2  Emergency Load Jettison 
     If the hook does not open properly, jettison the load by means of the 
     stand-by mechanical release control. 
3.3  System Failure 
     Indication of a fault : 
     - In Hover Flight : 
       In hooking or unhooking phase, release the load using the electrical 
       control. If the indication of the fault persists, abort the mission and 
       repair the system. 
     - In Forward Flight : 
       Avoid flying over built-up areas, perform a caution approach on the 
       nearest heliport then apply the previous procedure. 

PDF PAGE 587
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.15 
 
                        13-38 Page 7
 *01*
 
  
4  NORMAL PROCEDURE 
  
   Independently from the normal procedures provided below, the normal          
   procedures provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its supplements remain    
   applicable.                                                                  
  
   WARNING: TO REMOVE ANY STATIC ELECTRICITY FROM THE AIRCRAFT, IT IS             
            RECOMMENDED TO LET THE HOOK CREATE A GROUND CONNECTION                
            BEFORE THE CABLE IS GRASPED.                                        
  
4.1  Pre-flight checks 
  
     - Load indication on the NMDs set to zero .................. Checked. 
                                                                              
                                                                               R 
                                                                               R 
                                                                               R 
 
     - Operation of the controls and status indication 
       on the NMDs .............................................. Checked. 
       . Indication of an unloaded hook and 0 lb + 66 lb                        
         (0 kg + 30 kg) weight condition if no load is hooked on                
       or 
       . Indication of a load hooked-on condition and indication 
         of a weight value if a downward force greater than 
         28 lbf (13 daN) is applied (Post MOD 0725654).                         
  
     - Electrical and mechanical release controls ............... Operational. 
  
4.2  Takeoff 
  
     - Load hooked on  : takeoff vertically above the load, climbing very 
                         gradually. 
     - Cable tensioned : lift up the load vertically; do not assume forward 
                         flight before ensuring that the load is cleared away 
                         from any obstacle. 
  
4.3  Maneuvers                                                                 
  
     Perform gentle maneuvers, slow accelerations and decelerations, with no   
     steep turns; these recommendations are particularly applicable for the 
     transport of bulky loads offering high resistance to air. 
  
4.4  Laying down the load on the ground 
  
     - Check that the mission selector is in the SLING position. 
     - Lay down the load with no horizontal forward motion. 
  
     REMINDER : When the mission selector is not in the SLING position, 
                the sling information may remain displayed on the NMDs          
                but the load cannot be released using the electrical            
                control. 
  
  
  
  
                                                                            
 

PDF PAGE 588
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.15 
 
                        16-01 Page 8
 *01*
 
  
4.5  Load release 
  
     - Release the load, using the pushbutton located on the cyclic pitch 
       stick. 
       . Indication of an unloaded hook and 0 lb + 66 lb                        
         (0 kg + 30 kg) weight condition ........................ Checked.      
  
     CAUTION : BEFORE RESUMING FORWARD FLIGHT, CHECK THAT THE 
               LOAD IS ACTUALLY RELEASED. 
  
5  PERFORMANCE 
  
   Independently from the regulatory performance data provided below, the       
   performance provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its supplements remain   
   applicable.                                                                  
  
5.1  Regulatory performance data 
  
                                                                                
     Figure 4 hereafter specifies the maximum permissible takeoff weight       R 
     for external load carrying mission under the following conditions :        
  
     - BOTH ENGINES AT TAKEOFF RATING OR MAXIMUM TORQUE = 100% 
     - Zero wind 
     - No P2 air bleed 
     - MPAI bullets open. 
  
     NOTA : MPAI bullets closed : 
            . Performance data given in figure 4 must be reduced by 2%.        R 
  
     Releasing a load of 550 lb (250 kg) with respect to the maximum takeoff    
     weight given by figure 4 corresponds to a vertical climb at the rate of   R 
     100 ft/min approx., at sea level.

1  GENERAL 
  
   The installation includes : 
   - A release hook. 
   - A mechanical hooking assembly. 
   - An electrical control and monitoring system. 
   - A stand-by mechanical control. 
   - A placard (on RH side of the cockpit console). 
  
1.1  Release hook 
  
     The release hook is used to hook and relase a load. 
     As it is of swivel type, it leaves the load free to rotate. 
     It is fitted with : 
     - An electrical control release. 
     - A mechanical control release. 
  
1.2  Mechanical hooking assembly 
  
     The hook assembly consists of : 
     - an upper strut attached to the bottom of the MGB and secured to the 
       floor via a spherical bearing. 
     - a lower strut below the cabin floor, attached to the upper strut via an 
       universal joint which allow the load to swing within a 60° cone. 
  
     A bungee cord is provided to retract the release hook aft when it is 
     not loaded. 
  
1.3  Electric control and monitoring system (Figure 1) 
  
     This system includes : 
     - A mission selector switch. 
     - The pilot's and copilot's electric release controls. 
     - An interface unit. 
     - A load indication displayed on the NMD screens. 
     - A load sensor. 
     - Electrical coupling circuits. 
  
1.3.1  Mission selector switch 
  
       The mission selector switch, which is located on the cockpit overhead 
       panel, is accessible to both pilots, and energizes the system when 
       set to SLING. 
       When set to OFF this switch inhibits the electric load jettison 
       function. 
  
1.3.2  Electric jettison controls 
  
       The pilot and copilot are both provided with a normal jettison control 
       on their cyclic stick grips. This control is marked CARGO REL and is 
       protected by a cup shaped knob which acts as a switch guard. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
 

PDF PAGE 596

 532 A2 SUP.20 
 
                        16-01 Page 2
 *01*
 
  
1.3.3  Load indication and indicating system 
  
       The load and system status indications are displayed on the NMDs. 
       Two display modes are available : 
       - Mission selector switch set to SLING : 
         The sling information is displayed whatever the selection made from 
         the DCU, except for the aircraft weight selection which has priority. 
       - Mission selector switch set to OFF : 
         The sling information is displayed by selecting the SLING function 
         using the DATA key on the DCU. 
  
1.3.4  Interface unit 
  
       The interface unit displays the information from the load sensor on 
       the NMDs. 
                                                                               R 
 
1.3.5  Load sensor 
  
       The load sensor is incorporated in the release hook. 
  
1.4  Stand-by mechanical control 
  
     A trigger secured to the pilot's collective lever grip opens the hook in 
     emergency mode via a setting box. 
  
     NOTE : When the stand-by control is used, the load release hook remains 
            open and can only be locked if the setting box has been reset.

4  NORMAL PROCEDURE 
  
   Independently from the normal procedures provided below, the normal          
   procedures provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its supplements remain

            BEFORE THE CABLE IS GRASPED.                                         
   
4.1  Pre-flight checks 
  
     - Load indication on the NMDs set to zero .................. Checked. 
  
                                                                               R 
                                                                               R 
                                                                               R 
 
     - Operation of the controls and status indication                        
       on the NMDs .............................................. Checked.      
       . Indication of an unloaded hook and 0 lb ± 65 lb  
         (0 kg ± 30 kg) weight condition if no load is hooked on 
       or 
       . Indication of a load hooked-on condition and indication 
         of a weight value if a downward force greater than 
         15 daN (33 lbf) is applied 
  
     - Electrical and mechanical release controls ............... Operational. 
  
4.2  Take-off 
  
     - Load hooked on  : take off vertically above the load, climbing very 
                         gradually. 
  
     - Cable tensioned : lift up the load vertically; do not assume forward 
                         flight before ensuring that the load is cleared away 
                         from any obstacle. 
  
4.3  Maneuvers                                                                  
  
     Perform gentle maneuvers, slow accelerations and decelerations, with no    
     steep turns; these recommendations are particularly applicable for the 
     transport of bulky loads offering high resistance to air. 
  
4.4  Laying down the load on the ground 
  
     - Check that the mission selector is in the SLING position. 
     - Lay down the load with no horizontal forward motion. 
  
     REMINDER : When the mission selector is not in the SLING position, 
                the sling information may remain displayed  on the NMDs 
                but the load cannot be released using the electrical control.   
                                                                                
 
 

PDF PAGE 602
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.20 
 
                        16-01 Page 8
 
 
4.4  Laying down the load on the ground 
  
     - Check that the mission selector is in the SLING position. 
     - Lay down the load with no horizontal forward motion. 
  
     REMINDER : When the mission selector is not in the SLING position, 
                the sling information may remain displayed  on the NMDs 
                but the load cannot be released using the electrical control.   
                                                                                
4.5  Load release 
  
     - Release the load, using the push-button located on the cyclic pitch 
       stick. 
       . Indication of an unloaded hook and 0 kg ± 30 kg 
         (0 lb ± 65 lb) weight condition ........................ Checked. 
  
  
     CAUTION : BEFORE RESUMING FORWARD FLIGHT, CHECK THAT THE 
               LOAD IS ACTUALLY RELEASED. 
  
  
5  PERFORMANCE DATA                                                             
  
5.1  Regulatory performance data 
  
     Independently from the regulatory performance data provided below, the     
     regulatory performance data provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its    
     supplements remain applicable.                                             
  
     Figure 4 hereafter specifies the maximum permissible take off weight      R 
     for external load carrying mission under the following conditions :        
  
     - BOTH ENGINES AT TAKEOFF RATING OR MAXIMUM TORQUE = 100% 
     - Zero wind 
     - No P2 air bleed 
  
     Releasing a load of 250 kg (550 lb) with respect to the maximum take off 
     weight given by figure 4 corresponds to a vertical climb at the rate of   R 
     100 ft/min approx., at sea level.

FLIGHT MANUAL 
  
   
1  GENERAL  (Figure 1)  
   
   The installation consists of :  
   - A SPECTROLAB SX16-C searchlight,  
   - An electrical power supply,  
   - A control unit,  
   - An indicating system.  
   
1.1  Searchlight  (1)  
   
     The SPECTROLAB SX16-C searchlight is located forward of the RH cabin   
     door.  
     It is a powerful variable beam light which can be steered horizontally   
     and vertically.  
   
1.1.2  General Characteristics   
   
       - Xenon arc lamp                 1600 W power rating  
       - Maximum brightness             30 000 000 CANDELAS  
       - Beam width                     4° to 20°  
       - Horizontal adjustment          From 0° to 110° to the right  
       - Vertical adjustment            From 0° to 80° downwards  
   
1.2  Electrical Power Supply   
   
     The installation is supplied with 28 V dc from essential busbar PP3.  
     A circuit-breaker is provided to protect the installation.  
   
1.3  Control Unit  (2)  
   
     The control unit can be used form the cockpit or from the cabin.  
     It consists of :  
     - A MASTER power switch (3).  
     - A START push-button (4) to switch on the light.  
     - A FOCUS beam width selector (5).  
     - A 4-position DOWN-UP-RIGHT-LEFT steering control.  
   
1.4  Indicating   
   
     The LDG LT caption (7) on the landing gear control panel (8) lights up   
     when the searchlight is switched on.  
      
 
 532 A2 SUP.23
  
                   97-50 Page 1
 *01* 

PDF PAGE 618
   
 
FLIGHT MANUAL 
  
   
2  LIMITATIONS   
   
   The limitations provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its supplements     R  
   remain applicable.                                                          R  
   
3  EMERGENCY PROCEDURES   
   
   The emergency procedures provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its        R  
   supplements remain applicable.                                              R  
   
4  NORMAL PROCEDURES   
   
   Irrespective of the special procedures which are provided below, the          
   normal procedures provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its supplements   R  
   remain applicable.                                                          R  
   
   
     CAUTION  : AT DISTANCE OF LESS THAN 10 ft, AVOID DIRECTING THE LIGHT       R  
               BEAM TOWARD FLAMMABLE MATERIALS (FIRE HAZARD).                  R  
   
   
4.1  Operation   
   
     NOTE  : The searchlight must only be used with an electrical ground power   
            unit connected or with engines running.  
   
     - Check that the searchlight circuit breaker is engaged.  
     - Select the MASTER switch (3) to ON :  
       . the LDG LT caption (7) should light up,  
       . the fan should operate,  
     - Press the START push-button (4) for five seconds :  
       . the searchlight lights up.  
   
   
     CAUTION  : RELEASE THE START PUSH-BUTTON AS SOON AS THE ARC LAMP IS LIT IN   
               ORDER TO AVOID DAMAGING IT.  
   
     NOTE  : Ignition of the arc lamp may generate radio noise and interfere   
            temporarily with the on-board equipment.  
   
            - Use the 4-position control (6) to steer the light beam.  
            - Use the FOCUS selector (5) to adjust the beam width.  
   
4.2  Safety Devices   
   
     The power supply to the searchlight is automatically cut as soon as one   
     of the following conditions occurs :  
     - A transformer-rectifier fails,  
     - The NVG function is used,  
     - The emergency flotation equipment is deployed.

5  PERFORMANCE    
    
   Independently from the regulatory performance data provided below, the         
   performance data specified in the basic Flight Manual and its supplements      
   remain applicable.                                                             
    
5.1  Climbing Performance                                                          
    
     The twin-engine and single-engine rates of climb are given in the            
     table below :                                                                
    
                                                                               
     |                                                                      |     
     |                 CORRECTIONS OF AIRCRAFT PERFORMANCES                 |     
     |          WHEN THE SPECTROLAB SX16-C SEARCHLIGHT IS INSTALLED         |     
     |                                                                      |     
     |                                   |                                  |     
     |        TYPE OF PERFORMANCE        |           CORRECTIONS            |     
     |                                   |                                  |     
     |                                   |                                  |     
     | Twin-engine climb                 | -10 % of basic vertical speed    |     
     |                                   | with minimum of -50 ft/min       |     
     |                                   |                                  |     
     |                                   |                                  |     
     | Single-engine climb               | -10 % of basic vertical speed    |     
     |                                   | with minimum of -50 ft/min       |     
     |                                   |                                  |     
                                                                               R   
    
    
5.2  Maximum weight performance in CATEGORY A                                   R   
    
     The maximum weight performance in CATEGORY A given in the Supplements     R   
     used are to be reduced by values in table below :                         R   
    
                                                                               
     |                                                                      |  R   
     |                 CORRECTIONS OF AIRCRAFT PERFORMANCES                 |  R   
     |          WHEN THE SPECTROLAB SX16-C SEARCHLIGHT IS INSTALLED         |  R   
     |                                                                      |  R   
     |                                   |                                  |  R   
     |        TYPE OF PERFORMANCE        |           CORRECTIONS            |  R   
     |                                   |                                  |  R   
     |                                   |                                  |  R   
     |                                   | -10 kg per 10 kt of V.TOSS       |  R

1 GENERAL
1.1 Applicability
Theinformationgiveninthissupplementcoversthefuelcrossfeed
trainingprocedures.
1.2 BriefDescription
Thefuelcrossfeedsystemenablesbothenginestobesupplied
by a singlefuelsystem(LHor RHsystem)shouldonesystembe
damagedby projectiles.
Thecrossfeedfunctionisensuredby4 electrovalvesandlines
which:
- isolatethedamagedsystem,
- interconnectonesystemwiththeother.
A yellowX FEEDlightontheCautionAdvisoryPanelindicatesthe
systemcondition.
R
NOTE ThissystemisdescribedinmoredetailsinComplementary
FlightManual(Volume2) Section9.

2.1 Limitationsdueto Fuelsystem
X FEEDtrainingproceduresfromoneenginemustnotbeundertaken
ifon thisengine:
· bothfuelboosterpumpsarenotoperative,
· fuelfilterclogginglightisilluminatedonthefuelmanagement
panel,
· lowfuellevellightisilluminatedonthefuelmanagementpanel.
2.2 Inflightlimitations
- Maximumaltitude 8000ft
- Enginepower MaximumContinuousPower
- Maximumbankangle 30°

- Symptoms YellowX FEEDlightilluminatedonCaution
AdvisoryPanel,

CASE2 Whenreturningtonormaloperationfollowing
operationofX FEEDsystem.
Consequence AnomalyonX FEEDelectricalsupplysystem.

CONTINUEFLIGHT
CASE2 Itmaybe impossibleto relightrelevantengine,R
referto Section3.1paragraph6. R

thehelicopter.
Theweaponsareside-mountedinternallyon therightandlefthand
sidesof theforwardcabin.
Firingisperformedthroughtheopeningof theslidingwindows.
R
Eachassemblyconsistsessentiallyof thefollowingitems:
- a machine-gun
- an articulatedsupport
- a gunmountassembly
- a lockingsystem
- a stowedsystem. R

1.1.2 ArticulatedSupport
An articulatedsupport(15)hingedon a bracket(14)mountedon the R
cabinfloor,enablestheweaponto be stowedinsideor positionedin
theintermediateor externalfiringpositions.
A gunmountsupport(5)enablestheweaponto bemovedinazimuth.
Itstravelislimitedby twostops(9).Therighthandstopcanbe R
foldedawaytowithdrawtheweaponforstowage.
A cable(12)coupledwitha jack(16)retainsthesupportat therear.R
A safetypin(11)equippedwitha safetyflagis inserted R
whenevertheweaponcarriersareinstalled.
Itpreventsthesupportfrombeingmovedto theexternalposition.
1.1.3 Gun-Mount
A yoke(3)attachedto thegun-mountsupport(5)enablesthecradle
(1)to bemovedinelevation.
Itstravelislimitedbytwostopslocatedon thelefthandyoke.

whentheweaponcarriersarefitted.

attached.
Itisfittedon therighthandsidewithan ammunitionbox
receptacle(2)*,andatthebottomwitha collectingboxforthespent R
cartridgesandchainlinks(19). R

1.1.4 LockingSystem

windowledge,enablingthesupport(15)to bepivotedbetweenthe R
stowedor intermediateandexternalfiringpositions.
A lockingcontrolcover(8)preventstomovethehandleinstowage R
position. R

(15)is intheintermediateposition.
A safetystrap(17)fixedto a hooking-ringon thestructurekeeps R
themachine-guninthefireenvelope. R

afterhanging. R

1  GENERAL (Figure 1) 
  
1.1  Effectivity                                                                
  
     The weapon system is the set of equipment which enables firing of the gun 
     or launching of rockets from the helicopter within the flight envelope 
     specified in paragraph 2 of this Supplement. 
  
     The utilization in these conditions requires that the aircraft be fully    
     operational, in particular that all the following systems be               
     operational :                                                              
  
     . 4 SMD                                                                    
     . 2 FDC                                                                    
     . 2 PSU                                                                    
     . 2 HSU                                                                    
     . 2 DCU                                                                    
     . 2 AFCS                                                                   
     . 2 AFCP.                                                                  
  
     The GUNS-ROCKET WEAPON system is associated with the SEXTANT T100 OPTICAL  
     SIGHT system (see SECTION 9.90).                                           
  
                                    
    
     WARNING : AMMUNITION (ROCKET, MISSILE, ELECTRICALLY PRIMED AMMUNITIONS)    
               EXPLOSION OR DEPARTURE CANNOT BE EXCLUDED IN CASE OF LIGHTNING   
               STRIKE ON AIRCRAFT. DELIBERATE PENETRATION INTO METEOROLOGICAL   
               CONDITIONS WITH LIGHTNING RISK SHALL BE AVOIDED  WITH LIVE       
               AMMUNITION ONBOARD.   
 
     NOTE : When flying at proximity of military high power transmitters the   R 
            pilot shall adopt procedures as established per national military  R 
            authority.                                                         R
 

PDF PAGE 704
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44
99-04 Page 2
*01*
                                     Figure 1 

PDF PAGE 705
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44 
 
                        00-12 Page 3
 *01*
 
  
1.2  Description of the Installation (Figure 1) 
  
     The weapon system, represented in Figure 1 consists of : 
  
     - A Master Armament Safety Switch (MASS) (4).                              
     - A Safety Control Unit (SCU) (6).                                         
     - A Gun and Rocket Management Unit (GRMU) (10).                            
     - Two Actuator Electronic Control Units (AECU) (7).                        
     - A gun elevation control (3). 
     - A firing control (2). 
     - An emergency jettison control (1). 
     - Two weapon carriers, each fitted with an actuator (11) and a release  
       unit (12), which can accommodate : 
       . Two gun pods or, 
       . Two rocket launchers together with their associated electronic  
         control unit (LIU) or, 
       . A mix of the above weapons or, 
       . A single one of the above weapons. 
     - A protection kit for the assemblies including the multi-purpose         R 
       sponsons, the emergency floatation gear (if installed) and the main     R 
       landing gear units.                                                     R 
  
     The system is completed by a sight (5) which is described under  
     Section 9.90. 
  
1.3  Power Supply 
  
     The weapon system is supplied with 28 V dc from the on-board electrical  
     system. 
     For safety reasons, the MASS switch is provided to enable emergency or  
     selective jettisoning of the weapons and to enable the firing circuits  
     via the SCU and the cyclic control to be checked without having to  
     activate the GRMU. 
  
     The installation incorporates 11 protection circuit-breakers whose  
     positions are specified in Section 7.2. 
  
1.4  Lighting 
  
     The brightness adjustment of the control unit front panels is achieved  
     via the aircraft lighting control panel.

FLIGHT MANUAL

1.5  DESCRIPTION 
  
1.5.1  Master Armament Safety Switch (MASS) (Figure 2)                          
  
       A two-position ON-OFF switch (1) is provided for switching on the  
       firing and jettison circuits. 
       A safety clip fitted with a red flame, prevents it from being operated  
       inadvertently. 
  
1.5.2  Safety Control Unit (SCU) (Figure 3) 
  
1.5.2.1  Jettison Circuits Control WEAPON JETTISON PERMIT 
  
         Selective release is achieved via two push-buttons LEFT and RIGHT  
         (1), each protected by a safety switch guard. 
         Operation of either of these push-buttons releases the selected  
         weapon, irrespective of the master switch located on the pilot and  
         copilot collective sticks. 
  
         A green coloured caption light (6) with independent L and R circuits,  
         indicates to the crew the status of the LH and RH release circuits. 
         The caption is lit up when : 
         - the master armament safety switch (MASS) is selected ON,             
         - the weapon carrier release units are connected up and armed. 
         - a weapon is fitted. 
         When a weapon is released by pressing one of the push-buttons (1),  
         the caption light corresponding to the release unit extinguishes. 
         In the event of joint release, both caption lights extinguish. 
  
1.5.2.2  Firing Safety Device 
  
         Firing safety is provided by a two-position FIRE switch (4)  
         maintained by a switch guard. 
  
         A yellow caption light (5) split into L and R is allocated to 
         weapon safety. 
         For each weapon, each of the captions lights up when : 
         - the master armament safety switch (MASS) is selected ON,             
         - the firing safety switch (4) is on FIRE, 
         - the GRMU is selected ON, 
         - the corresponding weapon carrier is armed, 
         - the release circuits are connected up and armed, 
         - the AECU are operative.                                             R 
  
1.5.2.3  Control of AECU circuits SLVG SYS/TEST 
  
         A yellow coloured FAIL push-button (2), lights up when an item in the  
         actuation channel is unserviceable. The L and R captions (3) identify  
         the channel concerned. When the FAIL caption is lit, firing is  
         automatically inhibited. 
         During AECU testing, with master armament safety switch (MASS)         
         selected ON and GRMU selected OFF, the FAIL-L-R captions light up     R 
         when the button is pressed and the actuators can be operated  
         irrespective of the weapon fitted. When it is pressed again, the  
         captions extinguish. 
  
  
 

PDF PAGE 708
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44
97-50 Page 6
*01*
                                     Figure 4 

PDF PAGE 709
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44 
 
                        97-50 Page 7
 *01*
 
  
1.5.3  Gun and Rocket Management Unit (GRMU) (Figure 4) 
  
1.5.3.1  ON-OFF Switch (9) 
  
         The two-position ON-OFF switch is used for switching on and resetting 
         the GRMU (charging of the gun reset hydraulic accumulator). 
  
1.5.3.2  Weapon Selector (7) 
  
         The two-position switch is used to select the rocket (RKT) or gun 
         (GUN) weapon. 
  
1.5.3.3  Weapon Carrier Selector (5) 
  
         This selector is used for selecting the weapon carrier allocated to 
         the firing command : L (left), R (right) or LR (both). 
  
1.5.3.4  Zone Selector (1) 
  
         This is used in RKT mode only, for selecting the zones for the rocket 
         launchers. 
         When set on A or B, the rockets allocated to zone A or B are 
         selected. When set on AB, firing is performed on both zones. 
  
1.5.3.5  Firing Range Selector (3) 
  
         This is used in RKT mode only, the rotary switch provides 15 
         different ranges at 500 m intervals, between 0 and 7 km. 
         When 0 is selected, the display (10) indicates the minimum rocket 
         firing range. 
         This selector is not used with conventional rockets. 
         The AUTO position is not used on this version. 
  
1.5.3.6  Range Correction Selector +P/R -P/R (4) 
  
         In RKT mode : 
         This selector is ineffective with conventional rockets fitted. 
  
         In GUN mode : 
         This selector is used for resetting the RH gun (upwards) or the 
         LH gun (downwards). 
         It can only be used if the rotary mode selector is on position 1, 2, 
         4, 8 or ALL. 
 

PDF PAGE 710
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44 
 
                        97-50 Page 8
 *01*
 
  
1.5.3.7  Programming Selector (2) 
  
         This switch is used for loading parameters into the GRMU memories, 
         when specifying the weapon load, or when selecting the menu. 
         Selection of YES indicates that the data is accepted or that the 
         reply to the question in the display (10) is yes. 
         Selection of NO indicates that the data is modified or that the 
         reply to the question in the display (10) is no. 
  
1.5.3.8  MODE Selector 
  
         The different modes are selected via a rotary switch (8). 
  
         - RST mode : 
  
           Is used for programming into the GRMU memory, the type and number 
           of rockets loaded into each of the launcher zones, the number of 
           rounds attributed to each gun pod, and the type of menu. 
           Firing is inhibited when the selector is set on RST. 
  
         - MENU mode : 
  
           Used in RKT mode only, this is used for selecting the firing menu. 
           The display (10) shows a series of messages, indicating the menu 
           firing parameters and its identification number between 1 and 9. 
           In order to select a different menu, the programming selector (2) 
           must be operated until the required menu number is displayed. 
           Selecting NO switches to the next menu, selecting YES accepts the 
           menu currently displayed. 
           Firing in MENU mode is possible. If a menu has been accepted, 
           firing in accordance with the menu selection is continuous for as 
           long as the FIRE button is pressed in. 
           When a menu is selected, all the parameters for that menu 
           automatically replace the parameters determined by the actual 
           positions of the various push-buttons and switches. 
  
         - TEST mode : 
  
           With RKT selected, the TEST mode provides the following 
           information : 
           - Configuration of the rockets sub-system. 
           - Status of the weapon load remaining in the launchers. 
           - Report of the cyclic self-testing. 
           - Report of the maintenance tests. 
           With GUN selected, the TEST mode provides the following 
           information : 
           - Presence of the gun pods in the RH and LH stores positions. 
           - Status of the remaining weapon load in the gun pods. 
           - Report of the cyclic self-testing. 
           - Report of the maintenance tests. 
           This information appears in the form of successive messages shown 
           in the display (10). 
           Firing is inhibited when the selector is positioned on TEST. 
  
  
  
  
  
 

PDF PAGE 711
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44 
 
                        97-50 Page 9
 *01*
 
  
         - 1.2.4.8.ALL Mode 
  
           For manual firing control this selector must be set to any of  
           these positions :. 
  
         - With RKT selected, according to whether the selector is on 1,2,4 
           or 8, the number of rockets fired will be 1,2,4 or 8 on the rocket 
           launcher selected. When ALL is selected, firing is interrupted only 
           if the firing control is released or, if not, when there is no more 
           ammunition. 
         - In GUN mode, positioning the selector on 1,2,4 or 8 determines the 
           total number of rounds fired by each pod selected X 10. 
           When ALL is selected, firing is interrupted only if the firing  
           control is released or, if not, when there is no more ammunition. 
  
1.5.3.9  Display (10) 
  
         The 16-character display enables messages to be displayed such as 
         firing parameters or the results of self-testing. 
  
1.5.3.10  DIMMER Brightness Control (6) 
  
          This potentiometer is used for adjusting the brightness of the 
          display (10). 
  
1.5.4  Actuator Electronic Control Units (AECU) 
  
       Two control units located on the centre structure, receive and 
       transmit all the signals from the aircraft required to operate the 
       weapon carrier actuators. 
       Each actuator has its own separate AECU which elaborates the commands 
       intended for it. 
       The AECU operates under three modes : fixed elevation, gun and test. 
       - Fixed elevation mode corresponds to gun and rocket firing in 
         cruise flight. The fixed elevation setting is + 4°. 
       - Gun mode is used to transmit the elevation adjustment commands to 
         the gun, and when two guns are fitted, to align one gun relative to 
         the other. 
       - Test mode is actuated via the FAIL-L-R push-button on the 
         SCU. It is used for : 
         . Checking that the parameters inserted by the crew have been 
           loaded. 
         . Checking the overall operation of the actuator by pressing the gun 
           control, independently from the weapons selected. 
         . To bring back the actuator to within its normal operating range 
           when it reaches the end of travel stops. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
 

PDF PAGE 712
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44
00-12 Page 10
*01* 
1.5.5  Firing Control (Figure 5) 
       The firing control, which is common to the guns and to the rocket 
       launchers, is located on the pilot's cyclic control grip (4). 
       It consists of a micro switch (6) actuated by a fold-down firing 
       control (5). 
1.5.6  Master Release Control (Figure 5) 
       The master release button (1) is located on the pilot and copilot 
       collective sticks (2), and is protected by a SHEAR switch guard. 
       CAUTION : WHEN THE MASS IS SET TO ON, THE MISSION SELECTOR MUST NOT     R 
                 BE SET TO HOIST IN ORDER TO AVOID THE SIMULTANEOUS OPERATION  R 
                 OF THE PYROTECHNIC SHEAR OF THE HOIST CABLE.                  R 
       When the MASS unit is selected ON, pressing the master release 
       button results in opening of the release unit on each weapon carrier 
       and extinguishing of both green lights on the SCU. 
1.5.7  Gun Elevation Adjustment Control (Figure 5) 
       The gun elevation adjustment control (3) moved up or down is located  
       on the pilot's cyclic control grip (4). 
       It is used to control the motion of the actuator for each weapon 
       carrier via the corresponding AECU. 
                                     Figure 5 

PDF PAGE 713
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.44 
 
                        04-46 Page 11
 *01*
 
  
1.5.8  Weapon Carriers 
  
       Two removable weapon carriers are fitted forward of the cabin doors. 
       Each weapon carrier enables the carrying and release of a gun pod or a  
       rocket launcher via an attachment device incorporating a release unit. 
       The weapon elevation setting is provided by an actuator controlled by  
       signals received from the AECU. 
  
1.5.9  Gun Pod 
  
       The NC 621 gun pod is fitted with a 20 mm automatic gun, mounted on a  
       cradle fitted with flexible attachments. 
       The weapon is connected to a removable magazine with a total capacity  
       of 180 shells having the following characteristics :                   R 
       20x102, US M50 type shells fitted with brass sockets, complying with   R 
       Stanag 3585 standard and assembled with M12 or 23TE links complying    R 
       with MIL-L-12997F standard.                                            R 
       An electro-hydraulic re-arming system enables re-arming in flight in  
       the event of gun jamming during firing. 
       During firing, the spent cases and links are ejected from below the  
       pod. 
       The pod is attached beneath the release unit on the weapon carrier. 
       Its elevation setting is adjusted by the actuator. The elevation range  
       is from + 8° upwards to - 10° downwards relative to the aircraft  
       horizontal datum. In the cruise, the elevation setting is + 4° (GRMU  
       unit switched off). 
  
1.5.10  Rocket Launchers 
  
        The M159/A MOD 2-1 rocket launcher is designed for firing 2.75"  
        (70 mm) rockets : 
        . boosters, type FZ 68 mode 3 or mode 4                               R 
        . head types :                                                        R 
          - military : FZ 71                                                  R 
          - training : FZ 120                                                 R 
          - flash training : FZ 181                                           R 
  
        It is attached below the weapon carrier release unit. 
        Its elevation setting is + 4°. 
        It is connected to an electronic unit (LIU) mounted on the side of  
        the weapon carrier.

   Irrespective of the special limitations which are provided below, the        
   limitations provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its Supplements          
   remain applicable. 
2.1  Longitudinal Centre of Gravity Limits 
     The C of G datum is located at 4.67 m (183.85 in), forward of the centre 
     of the main rotor head. 
2.2  Lateral Centre of Gravity Limits  
     The datum is the aircraft plane of symmetry.

     - Maximum airspeed for firing gun and rockets, 
       with landing gear extended ................................   100 Kt. 
     - Maximum airspeed for weapons release ......................   100 Kt.

            . in descending flight with a rate of descent greater than

     - Maximum bank angle in turns ...............................   45°

     - Takeoff and landing envelope within the ZONE B of paragraph 2.1         R

     - The crew includes 2 pilots : 
       . Aiming and firing is performed by the pilot,                           
       . Flight safety is ensured by the copilot.                               
     - The height AGL whilst firing must not be less than 300 ft. 
  
  
3  EMERGENCY PROCEDURES 
  
    Irrespective of the special emergency procedures which are provided below,   
    the emergency procedures provided in the Basic Flight Manual and its         
    Supplements remain applicable.                                               
  
3.1  Weapon Release 
  
     This procedure is provided for lightening the aircraft in emergencies, 
     particularly before inflating the emergency floatation gear (when         R 
     installed).                                                               R 
     - Check that Vi is less than or equal to the release VNE. 
     - Master Armament Safety Switch (MASS) selected ON, green L and R PERMIT   
       lights lit on the SCU. 
  
       NOTE : When a weapon is carried by each pod, should either of the 
              L and R lights be lit, any weapon can still be released. 
  
3.1.1  General Weapon Release 
  
       - Press the master release button on the pilot or copilot collective 
         control stick. 
         . the L and R PERMIT lights should extinguish. 
  
3.1.2  Selective Release 
  
       - Operate the guarded switch toggle towards the side of the weapon to 
         be released. 
       - The L or R PERMIT caption extinguishes for the weapon released.

4.3  In Flight 
  
     Before firing, ensure that the flight and firing conditions are 
     compatible with the recommended power margins for firing in the hover or 
     in forward flight. 
  
     Hover 
  
     Check the power margin in the hover on the FLI : 
  
     - If the power margin is greater than or equal to 10%, manoeuvring about 
       the yaw axis is permissible without checking the power. 
  
     - If the power margin is less than 10%, monitor the power when carefully 
       manoeuvring about the yaw axis. 
  
     Forward flight 
  
     The recommended firing envelope is : 
  
     - with a power margin less than or equal to 5% : straight and level flight, 
  
     - with a power margin less than or equal to 10% : manoeuvring limited to 
       30° of bank.

         . side selector ..............................  on L or LR or R

      Safety Arming Procedure 
  
      At the end of the ammunition band, when less than 8 rounds are  
      remaining, the LOW caption flashes on the display during firing, then 
      the number of remaining rounds is displayed. 
  
      Perform another safety pass, during which the pilot fires at least once  
      after resetting the weapon in order to ensure that no rounds remain when 
      the aircraft returns to the flight pad. 
      The display should then indicate LG X 00 RG X 00, with flashing X. 
  
       After a Firing Mission 
       - on the pilot's cyclic grip ................  raise the fold-down  
                                                      firing trigger

         . ON/OFF switch ...........................  selected OFF

                                                      (inhibits the 
                                                      emergency release).

      Safety Arming Procedure 
  
      After firing training, a safety pass must be performed. To do this, the 
      crew must reprogram the GRMU using the RESET button and the procedure 
      described in the GRMU operating manual (selection : L+R, A+B, MODE 
      ALL), and fire all the rocket launcher tubes. 
  
      NOTE : The countdown of the display of the number of rockets 
             on the GRMU indicates the number of firing commands transmitted 
             to the launcher but not the number of rockets fired (firing 
             faults). 
  
      Firing Faults 
  
      - On the SCU : 
        . FIRE switch ...........................  guard lowered, L/R lights 
                                                   extinguished. 
  
        . any manipulating of the installation must only be performed on the 
          firing line. 
  
      - At landing, point the aircraft towards a safe area. 
  
      After the Firing Mission 
  
      - Pilot's grip firing trigger .............  Raised (in safety) 
  
      - on the SCU 
        . FIRE switch ...........................  guard lowered, L/R lights 
                                                   extinguished 
      - on the GRMU 
        . ON/OFF switch .........................  selected OFF 
  
      - on MASS unit ............................  switch OFF when instructed 
                                                   by the commanding officer 
                                                   (inhibits the emergency 
                                                   release).

     - sight .....................................  selected OFF

     - Check the shields on the helicopter as a whole.

1  GENERAL 
 
   This supplement contains the Electronic Warfare System (EWS) description 
   and operation. 
   Additional details in regards to the system operation and system  
   architecture are given in the Section 9.96 (Complementary Flight Manual). 
 
1.1  System equipment 
 
     The EWS (Electronic Warfare System) is a Self-Protection System, with 
     several threats detection capabilities (including radar, laser, missile 
     and CFD). The detection is performed by specific sensors for microwaves 
     (SAM), infrared (LWS), and ultraviolet (MAWS) electromagnetic radiation  
     and the countermeasure with chaff and flare payloads. 
 
     The EWS is composed of the following equipment: 
 
     - 1 EWC (Electronic Warfare Computer). 
 
     - Sensors and amplifiers, 
        
     - 1 EWS control panel located on pedestal (featuring power, cabin 
       firing authorization and PREVENTIVE pushbutton, 
 
     - 1 TWI (Threat Warning Indicator) with command and control features, 
 
     - 1 DTU (Data Transfer Unit) with one data transfer device (flashcard), 
 
     - On the overhead panel a Central Clear Switch and a MASS (Master Armament 
       Safety Switch),  
        
     - RF (Radio Frequency) cables to conduct the radio signals from the SAM 
       sensors to the receivers and processor. 
     
     - EWS audio control panel located just below the TWI. 
 
     - CFDS (Chaff and Flare Dispenser System) with a JETTISON pushbutton, 
 
     - 1 CFDCP (Chaff and Flare Dispenser Control Panel), 
 
     - 1 SPU (Safety Pin Unit), 
 
     - 1 SURVIVE pushbutton available on both cyclic stick grip, 
 
     - 4 SURVIVE pushbuttons installed over the cabin. 
 
1.2  Power Supply 
 
     The system is supplied by helicopter primary and secondary bus bars. 
     In case of power loss (generator off) the busses are automatically  
     reconfigured and power is provided by the battery. 
 
2 LIMITATIONS 
 
  The limitations specified in the Basic Flight Manual and in the Supplements 
  used remain applicable and are supplemented or modified by the following  
  limitations:
 

PDF PAGE 730
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.45 
 
                        15-44 Page 2
 *01*
 
2.1  Cartridges and magazine 
 
     WARNING : FLYING WITH EMPTY DISPENSER SHALL BE AVOIDED. 
               EMPTY MAGAZINES MUST NOT BE FITTED TO THE DISPENSER. MAGAZINE 
               SHALL BE FULLY LOADED WITH NEW OR FIRED PAYLOAD. 
     
     All expendables in a magazine must be of the same size (1" x 1" x 8"); 
     A maximum of two types of payloads can be mixed in a dispenser, obeying 
     a predefined order. The two dispensers must have the same payload  
     configuration (MAG ID).  
  
     The allowed cartridge types are: 
     - Chaff type 1- RR-170 cartridges using BBU-35/B squibs; 
     - Flare type 1- M206 cartridges using BBU-35/B squibs. 
 
2.2  Operation 
 
     WARNING : WHEN FIRING FLARES, THE LIGHT INTENSITY OF INFRARED DECOYS CAN  
               CAUSE MOMENTARY GLARE ON THE NVG GOGGLES. 
 
               TO RELEASE CHAFFS, THE AIRCRAFT DOORS, AIRCRAFT WINDOWS AND THE  
               MULTI-PURPOSE AIR INTAKES SHALL BE CLOSED. 
 
     CAUTION 1 : IT IS NOT PERMITTED TO FLY INSIDE THE CHAFF CLOUD. IN CASE THAT 
                 HAPPENS, ALL THE RELATED MAINTENANCE/INSPECTION MUST BE  
                 PERFORMED (MULTI-PURPOSE AIR INTAKE, AIR CONDITIONING, CABIN, 
                 ETC.).     
 
     CAUTION 2 : CHAFF FIRING : 
                 CARTRIDGES ARE OF TYPE RR-170 AND MUST BE  
                 LAUNCHED WITH A MINIMUM FORWARD SPEED OF 50 KIAS. 
                 DO NOT FLY THROUGH CHAFF CLOUDS. IN CASE OF SUSPICION OF  
                 CHAFF INGESTION,THE HELICOPTER AIR INLETS (ENGINES, AIR  
                 CONDITIONNING, CABIN, ETC.) MUST BE INSPECTED FOR CHAFF 
                 CONTAMINATION. 
                
                 FLARE FIRING : 
                 CARTRIDGES ARE OF TYPE M-206. 
                 THE SQUIBS TO BE USED WITH RR-170 AND M-206 CARTRIDGES MUST 
                 BE BBU-35/B.  
 
     CAUTION 3 : IN FLIGHT, RISK INDUCED BY MISFIRE OF CARTRIDGES SHALL BE  
                 COVERED BY SPECIFIC OPERATIONAL PROCEDURES.  
 
     CAUTION 4 : THE PAYLOAD INVENTORY CAN BE ERRONEOUS; SOME CARTRIDGES MAY 
                 NOT BE TAKEN INTO ACCOUNT. THIS RESULT FROM DIRTY  
                 ACCUMULATION BETWEEN DRIVER AND PIN. 
                 IT IS RECOMMENDED TO FLY WITH MAGAZINE WITH FULL PAYLOAD 
                 (EMPTY CARTRIDGES) IN ORDER TO MINIMIZE DIRT ACCUMULATION.
 

PDF PAGE 731
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.45 
 
                        15-44 Page 3
 *01*
 
3 EMERGENCY PROCEDURES 
 
3.1 Integrated self-protection system failure 
 
    Consequences : 
    . The EWS FAILURE audio will be heard, 
    . The mission status indicators on the TWI are all RED. 
      
    Actions :  
 
    On the CFDCP: 
    - CFSS...............................................Check OFF, guarded. 
    On the EWS control panel : 
    - POWER...........................................OFF and then ON again. 
    If the system recovers, continue the mission while monitoring the EWS 
    closely. 
 
    If the failure condition persists, the aircrew must use the BACKUP mode  
    of the EWS to ensure emergency countermeasure dispensing under MANUAL 
    CONTROL. 
 
3.2 Sub-system failure 
 
    CAUTION : FOR MISSILE WARNING DETECTION FUNCTION, IN UV DENSE  
              ENVIRONNEMENT THE MWSS CAN BECOME UNSERVICEABLE AND SYSTEM  
              REPORT BIT FAILURES. IF FAILURES REMAIN PRESENT FOR LONGER THAN 
              15 SEC., THE SYSTEM SHOULD BE RESTARTED. IF FAILURE CONDITION 
              PERSISTS AFTER THE RESTART, THE SYSTEM HAS TO BE CONSIDERED AS 
              UNSERVICEABLE. 
 
    Consequences : 
 
    . TWI mission status indicators, i.e. R, M, L or C with red background : 
      critical failure detected, 
 
    . TWI mission status indicators, i.e. R, M, L or C with yellow  
      background degraded functionality detected, 
 
    . Failure tones (Audio). 
 
    Actions : 
 
    On the TWI, determine which sub-system/sector has failed on the  
    maintenance status screen. 
 
    - Visually scan the affected sector for clues to any threat. Be prepared 
      to act immediately when a threat is recognized. It is possible to  
      receive warnings, generated from the other serviceable sub-systems, 
      provided the threat is valid for the other sub-systems as well. 
 
    - Press the manual Chaff and Flare firing button when the situation  
      warrants such action to be taken.

3.4 CFDS Failure procedures 
 
    - On the maintenance status screen on the TWI, determine if the CFDC  
      card in the EWC and/or dispenser has failed. 
 
    - On the CFDS inventory page, of the TWI, see which payloads are still  
      available. 
    
    If the CFDS has failed, use the CFDS in BACKUP MODE. To revert to the  
    BACKUP MODE, the following procedures are applicable : 
 
    - CFDCP Mode selector................................................BKP. 
 
    - CFSS switch......................................................ARMED. 
 
    - Dispense button............................................As required. 
 
3.5 Jettison procedure 
 
    The jettison of all decoy payloads is only possible in flight, when EWS 
    is ON and all CFD safety removed (SPU, MASS, CFSS). 
 
    Jettison is available for all mode setting selected on CFDCP. 
     
    On the CFDCP, 
    - CFSS switch......................................................ARMED. 
    
    - Jettison pushbutton.................................Press during 3 sec. 
 
    Wait 10 seconds before JETTISON to confirm sequence : 
 
    - Jettison pushbutton.................................Press during 3 sec. 
 
4  NORMAL PROCEDURES 
 
   The normal procedures specified in the Basic Flight Manual and in the  
   Supplements used remain applicable and are supplemented or modified by the 
   following normal procedures: 
 
   WARNING 1 : THE CHAFF OR FLARE EXPLOSION OR FIRE UNTIMELY IGNITION CANNOT 
               BE EXCLUDED IN CASE OF LIGHTNING STRIKE ON THE HELICOPTER. 
               DELIBERATE PENETRATION INTO METEOROLOGICAL CONDITIONS  
               PRESENTING LIGHTNING RISK SHALL BE AVOIDED WHEN THE HELICOPTER 
               IS EQUIPPED WITH CHAFF OR FLARE. IN CASE OF UNEXPECTED  
               ENCOUNTER OF SUCH CONDITIONS CHAFF AND FLARE SHALL BE  
               JETTISONED.    
 
   WARNING 2 : WHEN EQUIPPED WITH CHAFF OR FLARE CARTRIDGES THE HELICOPTER 
               SHALL ALWAYS LAND AND TAKEOFF ON AREAS WITHOUT UNPROTECTED 
               EXPLOSIVE MATERIAL (AMMUNITION, FLAMMABLE FLUID). 
               PEOPLE SHALL BE OUT OF THE RANGE OF THE CARTRIDGES. 
               ON GROUND SYSTEM SHOULD BE NON-ARMED. 
               HOWEWER, IF OPERATIONAL PROCEDURES REQUIRE SYSTEM IS ARMED ON  
               GROUND, IT SHALL BE INSURED THAT THERE IS NO GROUND PERSONNAL OR 
               UNPROTECTED EXPLOSIVE MATERIAL IN THE RANGE OF THE CARTRIDGES. 
 
   
 

PDF PAGE 734
FLIGHT MANUAL 
 532 A2 SUP.45 
 
                          15-44 Page 6
 *01* 
 
WARNING 3 : THE CHAFF AND FLARE DISPENSER SYSTEM SHALL BE OFF (ALL SAFETY 
            SWITCHES AND EWS OFF) DURING IN-FLIGHT REFUELING. 
 
  
CAUTION 1 : WHEN FLYING WITH CHAFF AND FLARE CARTRIDGES AT PROXIMITY OF 
            MILITARY HIGH POWER TRANSMITTERS, THE PILOT SHALL ADOPT  
            PROCEDURES AS ESTABLISHED PER NATIONAL MILITARY AUTHORITY. 
 
 
CAUTION 2 : DURING FLARE CARTRIDGE DISPENSE THE MISSILE WARNING SYSTEM CAN 
            BE MOMENTARILY SATURATED.  
 
 
CAUTION 3 : THE READABILITY OF THE RADAR THREATS ON THE THREAT WARNING 
            INDICATOR (TWI) MAY BE HAMPERED IN THE PRESENCE OF MULTIPLE 
            THREATS. 
            RECOMMANDATION : 
            SPECIAL ATTENTION HAS TO BE TAKEN DURING LIBRARY SET UP. 
            - THE OPERATOR SHOULD PREPARE SUB-LIBRARIES WITHOUT UNKNOWN 
              CATEGORIES. 
            - THE OPERATOR SHOULD ASSIGN SHORT LABELS TO THE THREATS. 
            - PROPER ASSIGNMENT OF LETHALITY LEVEL TO THE DIFFERENT RADAR 
              THREATS (RADIATED POWER, TRANSMITTER TYPE) IN THE LIBRARY 
              IMPROVES THREAT DISCRIMINATION AND SEPARATION ON THE TWI.  
 
CAUTION 4 : WHEN ARMAMENT IS INSTALLED ON BOARD THE HELICOPTER,FIRING CAN 
            CAUSE MISSILE DETECTION OR JAMMING OF MISSILE WARNING SYSTEM. 
            IT IS RECOMMENDED TO SET, BEFORE FIRING, THE CHAFF AND FLARE  
            DISPENSER IN SEMI-AUTOMATIC MODE IN ORDER TO AVOID POTENTIAL 
            AUTOMATIC DISPENSE ON MISSILE. 
 
CAUTION 5 : WHEN ON BOARD LASER EMITTED IS USED, THE ELECTRONIC WARFARE 
            SYSTEM CAN DETECT THE SIGNAL. IN ORDER TO PREVENT AUTOMATIC 
            DISPENSE PENDING TO LASER DETECTION LIBRARY, IT IS RECOMMENDED 
            TO SET PRIOR THE CHAFF AND FLARE IN SEMI-AUTOMATIC MODE. 
 
CAUTION 6 : FALSE MISSILE WARNINGS MAY BE TRIGGERED BY UV SOURCES IN THE 
            ENVIRONNEMENT AROUND THE HELICOPTER (LIKE OIL REFINERIES FLARE 
            STACKS, ARC WELDING, AIRCRAFT LANDING LIGHTS).IN ORDER TO 
            PREVENT AUTOMATIC DISPENSE OF CHAFF AND FLARE UPON FALSE  
            MISSILE WARNING, IT IS RECOMMENDED TO SELECT SEMI-AUTOMATIC 
            MODE, IF PRESENCE OF SOURCES IS LIKELY. 
4.1  Checks 
 
4.1.1 External checks  
 
     . Safety pin on SPU.......................................Check installed, 
     . Missile Approach Warning Sensors (MAWS).... properly secured, clean and 
                                                   undamaged, 
     . Laser Warning Sensors (LWS)....... properly secured clean and undamaged, 
     . Spiral Antenna Module (SAM)............. properly secured and undamaged, 
     . Chaff and Flare dispensers (CFD)........ properly secured and undamaged, 
     . Magazines (MAG)......................... properly secured and undamaged, 
                                            and loaded with Chaff and/or Flare. 
4.1.2 Internal checks 
 
      . EWS control panel POWER pushbutton.................................OFF. 
      Connect power to the aircraft, 
      . CFSS on CFDCP....................... Visual check and properly secured. 
      . TWI display...............................................Visual check.

4.1.3 Check before starting engines 
 
      - MASS switch........................................................OFF. 
      On the CFDCP, 
      - CFSS.......................................................Guarded,OFF. 
      - CFDS mode selector................................................STBY. 
      - JETT pushbutton............................................Guarded,OFF. 
      On the EWS control panel, 
     - POWER pushbutton....................................................OFF.  
 
4.1.4 Check after starting engines 
 
      NOTE 1 : System operating : 
               The core avionics shall be correctly initialized before EWS 
               start-up. Once the system has finished its power Build In Test 
               the operator shall check the Pilot Failure List even if the 
               sub-system status is declared as nominal to ensure that no  
               failure is reported. 
 
      NOTE 2 : System start-up : 
               Do not operate the system with helicopter on DCGPU (overvoltage 
               limitation). Switch it ON only in battery or ACGPU modes or 
               after engine starting sequence. 
               During engine starting, network reconfiguration and with high 
               electrical consumer (involving a voltage lower than 18 Vdc), 
               the EWS will perform a warm start and return fully operational 
               after 50 sec. 
               As a consequence it is recommended to switch on the EWS only 
               after engines start-up. 
 
      NOTE 3 : System start-up condition in hot conditions : 
               In hot environmental condition, it is recommended to open the 
               cargo doors before EWS start-up. 
 
      - EWS control panel POWER pushbutton.................................ON. 
 
      - CFDS mode indicator..............................................STBY. 
      
      - TWI backlighting intensity................................As required. 
       
      - TWI Mission status page...............................Check all green, 
                                                          check library UDFXX. 
 
        NOTE : Library can be changed only with CFDCP in STBY mode. 
 
      - TWI / MNT page..................Check absence of message (page empty). 
 
4.1.5 Before take-off 
     
      - Safety pin in the SPU.......................Request removal and store 
                                                        on-board the aircraft. 
     
      On the CFDCP : 
      - ARM, RDY and DSP indicators.......................................OFF. 
      Ensure that the CFDS is safe.

4.1.6 In flight 
 
      CAUTION : WHEN ANTENNA ROTATION PERIOD (ARP) IS ACTIVATED, THE RADAR

                SELECTED FOR THE ARP AND STOPS MONITORING ALL OTHER BANDS. 
                ANY THREAT OUTSIDE THE BAND SELECTED FOR THE ARP WILL NOT BE 
                DETECTED NOR DISPLAYED.

      the SPU. 
 
      - Safety pin....................................................Install. 
      
      - EWS control panel.................................................OFF.

1  GENERAL 
  
     The "STAR SAFIRE 380-HD" FLIR system is an observation system based on  
     an Infra-Red (IR) camera, a TV camera and a low light camera , with full 
     high definition imaging for a wide range of critical night and day  
     missions. 
  
     For a complete description of the FLIR system, refer to section 9.91. 
  
2  LIMITATIONS 
  
   The limitation specified in the Basic Flight Manual and the relevant 
   supplements remain applicable and are supplemented by the following  
   limitations :  
  
2.1 Laser Pointer (LP)  
   
    WARNING: 1) THE LASER POINTER (LP) IS NOT EYESAFE. IT PRODUCES AN  
                INVISIBLE LASER BEAM WHICH CAN CAUSE PERMANENT LOSS OF 
                EYESIGHT. IT MUST NEVER BE POINTED DIRECTLY AT PERSONS  
                OR TARGETS WITH PERSONS, WHETHER IN FLIGHT OR ON THE  
                GROUND. 
  
             2) ALL SAFETY DEVICES RELATIVE TO THE LASER POINTER (LIU :  
                KEY OFF AND UHCU : LASER SWITCH DISARM) MUST REMAIN IN PLACE 
                AS LONG AS THE FOLLOWING CONDITIONS ARE NOT MET: 
 
                - AT LEAST ONE CREW MEMBER CAN SEE THE FLIR LASER BEAM DURING 
                  ALL THE FIRING. 
                   
                - THE FLIR LINE OF SIGHT IS ALWAYS UNDER THE CREW'S CONTROL. 
                - NO PERSON OR TARGET WITH PERSONS, MUST BE IN THE FIRING OR 
                  SAFETY ZONE (REFER TO SAFETY DISTANCES TABLE IN PARAGRAPH  
                  2.2.  
                - NO SIGNIFICANT OFFSET BETWEEN THE FLIR LINE OF SIGHT AND THE 
                  LASER BEAM. 
 
             3) IN CASE OF LOSS OF CONTROL OF THE FLIR LINE OF SIGHT, THE LASER 
                POINTER MUST BE DEACTIVATED BY ALL MEANS AVAILABLE (LIU : KEY  
                OFF AND UHCU : LASER SWITCH DISARM) . 
 
2.2 Safety range 
  
                                                           
     |                     |  LASER  |  EYE SAFE          |  
     |                     |  POWER  |  LIMIT (meter)     | 
     |                     |         |                    |  
     | Unaided Eye         | 150 mW  |      600 m         |  
     |                     |         |                    | 
     |                     |         |                    |  
     | 7x50 mm Binoculars  | 150 mW  |     4200 m         | 
     |                     |         |                    | 
     |                     |         |                    |

     |                     |         |                    |

3  EMERGENCY PROCEDURES 
  
   The emergency procedures specified in the Basic Flight Manual and the  
   relevant supplements remain applicable. 
 
   In case of unexpected or improper illumination of LIU ARM light : 
 
   CAGE/STOW switch on the UHCU.........................................STOW, 
 
   PWR ON/OFF switch on the UHCU.........................................OFF. 
  
4  NORMAL PROCEDURES 
  
   The normal procedures specified in the Basic Flight Manual and the  
   relevant supplements remain applicable. 
 
   CAUTION 1 : TO PROTECT THE LENSES FROM DAMAGE, THE SYSTEM SHOULD BE IN 
               STOW POSITION DURING TAXI, TAKEOFF AND LANDING. 
   
   CAUTION 2 : DURING TAXI, TAKEOFF AND LANDING, THE LASER POINTER MUST BE SET 
               TO OFF (LIU : KEY OFF AND UHCU LASER SWITCH DISARM). 
               THE EOS MUST BE IN STOW POSITION. 
  
4.1 External checks   
 
    Turret-FLIR unit............................................visual check, 
    System cables..........................................securely attached. 
    Manually rotate the gimbals : 
    Optics windows.......................................Clean and undamaged. 
 
4.2 Checks before starting engines  
 
    PWR ON/OFF switch on the UHCU.................................Check, OFF.

4.3 After starting engines  
 
    Start up the EOS (Power ON on UHCU) when the on-board electrical power 
    supply is available (2 GTM operating and ground power unit removed).  
 
4.4 In-flight operation 
 
    PWR ON/OFF switch on the UHCU..................................Check, ON, 
 
    Built-In-Test..........................................Automatically run. 
 
    INIT appears in the System Data Area in the lower right-hand screen once  
    the symbology becomes visible. When the system has completed the  
    initialization process, CAGE appears in this position. The system moves  
    in azimuth and elevation to reach to the CAGE position. 
    If a fault is detected during the BIT test, the fault message appears in  
    the BIT menu. 
 
4.5 Checks prior to landing 
 
    CAUTION : MAKE SURE THE SYSTEM IS OFF PRIOR TO SHUTTING DOWN ANY PLATFORM 
              POWER SOURCES. SHUTTING DOWN THE PLATFORM POWER WHILE THE SYSTEM 
              IS ON COULD DAMAGE THE SYSTEM. 
 
    Menu selector.......................................................STOW. 
 
    The Turret-FLIR moves to its stowage position. 
 
    NOTE : To protect the lenses from damage, allow sufficient time  
           (up to 10 seconds) for the TFU to move to the STOW position and 
           make sure STOW appears in the system data area of the display, 
           before turning power off. 
 
    PWR ON/OFF switch on the UHCU........................................OFF. 
 
4.6  After landing 
 
     CAUTION : MAKE SURE THE SYSTEM IS OFF PRIOR SHUTTING DOWN THE HELICOPTER 
               POWER SOURCE. SHUTTING DOWN THE HELICOPTER POWER WHILE THE EOS  
               IS ON COULD DAMAGE THE SYSTEM.

3 EMERGENCYPROCEDURES
Irrespectiveof thespecificemergencyproceduresgivenbelow,the
emergencyproceduresintheBasicManualandthedifferentsupplements
remainapplicable.
3.1 Failureof an Engine
Intheeventof anemergency,theweightof theaircraftcanbe
reducedwhenit includesjettisonableequipment:
- cabinfueltank,
- armement,
- external loads.
Referto thecorrespondingsupplements.

4 NORMALPROCEDURES
Irrespectiveof thenormalproceduresgivenbelow,thenormal
proceduresof theBasicManualandthedifferentsupplementsremain
applicable.
4.1 PilotingPrecautions
At thealternategrossweight,inparticular,thepilotingmustbe
flexibleandthepilotinputson theflightcontrolsmustbe free
frombrutalityandjerks,thepoweradjustmentsmustbeprogressive.
4.2 Pre-FlightCheck
- Checktheconditionof thetires.
- Checktheinflationpressureof thetires:
.Mainlandinggear .
. Noselanding gear .
131psi(9bars)
101psi(7bars)
- Checkthatallthesystemsareintheirnominaloperating
condition.
NOTE: Anytestthatdoesnotgivesatisfactoryresultsimpliesthat
a maintenanceactionmustbecarriedout.
4.3 TakeoffProcedures
4.3.1 SpecificProceduresatAGW
- Aligntheaircraftwitha suitablestrip(inorderto avoidnose R
landinggeartouchdown). R
- Nosewheellockedcentered.
- Performa checkhoverflightIGE5 ft,thenfolloweitherof thetwo
followingprocedures:
TakeofffromtheIGE5 ft
Followthesameprocedureastherecommendedtakeoffprocedurein
theBasicFlightManual,butfromtheIGE5 ftandlimitingthe
nosedownattitudeto 5° (in2 s) insteadof 10°(in2 s).
. RunningTakeoff
Aircraftalignedwiththestripaxis.
R
Cycliccontrolstickcentered,graduallyapplythemaximum
continuouspower.
Lettheaircraftacceleratebycontrollingtherollwiththe
cyclicleverandtheyawwiththetailrotorcontrolpedals.
Whentheaircraftliftsoff,adjustthepowerwithoutexceeding
themaximumtakeoffpowerandcontinueacceleratingup toVy,
stayingneartheground.

Usetherecommendedapproachpathwithouttryingtomarkthehover

1 GENERAL
Theinstallationisdesignedto enabletrainedpersonnelto be lowered
froma helicopterinhoverflight,withthehelicopterequippedwitha
shortfootstep.
Itisusedto reachparticularlyinaccessibleplacesandintheshortest
timepossible.
Theinstallationcomprises
- thehoistarm(1).
- onereleaseunit (2) underthehoistopenstheropinghook
viaa mechaniccontrol(3) securedby a safetypin (4).

2.1 Utilizationenvelope
Thefastropeinstallationisqualified. R
- Fastropingoperationislimitedwithhoverflight. R
- Windlimitationson therope
Inhoverflight 20 kt.
Inlevel flight A relativewindof25 ktfromthefront
A relativewindof 15 ktfromtherear
A relativewindof 35 ktfromtheleft
A relativewindof 15 ktfromtheright
- Maximumpermissibleload : 573lb (260kg),providingthatthebraking
duringthedescentisas constantas possible.

4.1 Preparationof themission
Installthefastropingequipmentas specifiedintheMaintenance
Manual.
R
Verifythepresenceof thesafetypin.
Theropemustbecoiledintheconcentriccirclesatthemiddleof the
cargocompartment.
CAUTION PILOTMUSTTAKESPECIALCAREOFACCIDENTALUNLOADEDROPE
BEHAVIOUR,PARTICULARLYON DOORSOPENING.
- Alwaysuse:
. thehoistoperator'ssafetybeltandtheinterphoneheadsetprovided
fortheoperator/assistant,
. safetystrapswhenflyingwiththedoorsopen.
NevercarryoutfastropingoperationiftheAFCSisnotoperative. R
4.2 Fastropingoperations R
Hoverat a heightof20 ft lowerthantheropelenght·
Thepilotgivestheauthorizationforfastropingoperationsto the R
operator/assistant.
Theoperator'sassistant
Pushestheropeoutsidethehelicopter,inonesinglemovement.
troughttheRH sideexit.
Isobligedto keepthetotalropelengthinviewpermanently.
Ropesthefirstrescuer.
Announcesto thepilot: Readyto letdown.
Then: Letdown.
Therescuerscomesonebyoneto thedooranddescendsontherope.

. Pullsuptheropeorjettisonstherope. R
